# Release notes

HeatCaller is in beta. Version numbers follow **a.b.c**: *b* goes up when a whole feature lands, *c* for fixes and smaller additions.

## Unreleased

- **The program and results, for the night as clubs run it (12 November).**
    - **A protest window after every round.** Each qualifying round of a class, and each main, now has its own window: the announcer names the round ("That's Q1 done for Stock..."), the **Big Screen**'s finished board says "PROVISIONAL UNTIL 20:42", and a query asked inside a window goes to the top of **Driver Requests**, marked **In the protest window**. The finals still wait only for the last round's. See [The protest window](race-day/program-and-mains.md#the-protest-window).
    - **Stop early, finals too.** With an out-by time, **We're Done After This Round...** also offers the finals 2 minutes shorter, or one main fewer per class, with the minutes each saves, applied when the finals are built. See [We're done after this round](race-day/program-and-mains.md#were-done-after-this-round).
    - **Skip a driver, scored DNS.** Right-click the Next Up bar, or a driver on the board, for **Skip in this race (DNS)**. An approved **Car broken - skip me** from a phone does the same. Race History lists them under the race's finishers as DNS. **Undo** puts them back. See [Skip a driver in the next race](race-control/running-a-race.md#skip-a-driver-in-the-next-race-dns).
    - **The marshal rule.** **A marshal who doesn't turn up:** on **Setup**: nothing, loses their best qualifying result (the default), or a time penalty in their next race. A no-show from **Someone's missing...** applies it, **Qualifying Standings** and the event report say why, and clicking the line again takes it off. See [Marshals in place](race-day/program-and-mains.md#marshals-in-place).
    - **By race number.** **First round seeded:** has **By race number**, the BRCA way.
    - **Races off between a driver's races:** on **Setup** (0 to 3, 1 as before) for the build and Auto start's rest. **Move Drivers Between Heats** and dragging the running order warn before saving a change that puts a driver closer: "Max Power would race in Q2 Heat 1 and Q2 Heat 2 back to back."

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC. Nothing new on the relay.

- **Loose ends from setting up the night (12 November).**
    - **Templates on nights.** **When...** beside **Templates:** on **Race Day → Setup** sets the nights a template is for, such as every Tuesday or a monthly date. On those nights **Next Steps** offers **Start tonight's Tuesday club night**.
    - **Racing's off, by push too.** **Call It Off...** can also send a push to every phone signed up for the club's alerts.
    - **A driver's second transponder.** A car counts as heard tonight when any transponder its driver owns crosses the loop, so a driver running one transponder per car isn't listed as not heard.

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC. The push **needs the relay redeployed** (`fly deploy --ha=false`).

- **Hand the desk over, and bring a club over (12 November).**
    - **Hand the Desk to a Phone...** on the **Auto start** switch's right-click menu shows the phone remote's QR code for a deputy to run the starts while you race. The remote now has **START NOW** and **MARSHALS IN** too. See [Phone remote](screens/phone-and-web.md).
    - **Desk View...** on **Who's Racing** locks the laptop to check-ins, papers and money for a volunteer, until it's unlocked with the PIN you chose. See [Race Day → Who's Racing](race-day/events-and-entries.md#race-day-whos-racing).
    - **Import a List...** on the first-run wizard's **Drivers** step brings in a club's drivers, transponders and classes from an RCSignup or LiveTime export, a LiveRC entry list or a spreadsheet, adding any class it names.

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Night templates (12 November).** **Templates:** on **Race Day → Setup** keeps a night's settings under a name, such as "Tuesday club night" or "Points round". **Start Tonight From It** starts a race day with them, for a club that runs more than one kind of night. See [Race Day → Setup](race-day/events-and-entries.md#about-the-day). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **New to racing (12 November).** Tick **New to racing** on a driver's profile, and add their **Mentor:**. **Who's Racing** shows "novice, mentor Al Gorithim" beside their name, or "first night" for a driver whose profile was made today. The announcer welcomes new drivers once a night, in the gap before their first race: "A big welcome to Max Power, racing with us for the first time tonight - give them room out there." See [Drivers and classes](race-day/drivers-and-classes.md). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Ready to race, and the drivers' meeting (12 November).** Once everything is set up, the **Ready to race** card on **Next Steps** offers **Ready to Race...**: a checklist for before the first race. It covers the decoder hearing cars, the phone pages, the club website, the announcer's voice, the heats posted, every car heard, papers, a recent backup and the drivers' meeting. Lines tick themselves, and each has a button to the screen that sorts it. **Meeting Sheet...** writes the drivers' meeting from tonight's day, ready to print or have the announcer read out. See [The Ready to race checklist](race-day/program-and-mains.md#the-ready-to-race-checklist). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Before the night: announce it, call it off, and who's coming; papers at the desk (12 November).**
    - **Announce the next race night.** **Announce...** on **Race Day → Setup** puts it on the phones' Home page: the date, the classes on, when entries close and the first race. It comes down by itself the morning after. See [Race Day → Setup](race-day/events-and-entries.md#about-the-day).
    - **Call it off.** **Call It Off...** shows the night as off on the phones, with your message, and can email every regular through the club's mailbox.
    - **"I'm coming" from the phone.** On **Me**, a driver ticks their classes and taps **I'm coming**. **Who's Racing** then says "12 said they're coming · 9 here · still to come: ...". See [Who said they're coming](race-day/events-and-entries.md#who-said-theyre-coming).
    - **Waivers and memberships.** Tick **Ask every driver for a signed waiver** or **Check memberships** on **Setup** and **Who's Racing** gets a **Papers** column ("Waiver missing", "Membership expired 3 Oct"). Click it to record them. **Post the heats** lists anyone not in order. See [Papers: waivers and memberships](race-day/events-and-entries.md#papers-waivers-and-memberships).

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC. The next-night card and **I'm coming** **need the relay redeployed** (`fly deploy --ha=false`); papers and the emails work without it.

- **Start tonight in one press, and late check-ins that sort themselves (12 November).**
    - **Start tonight like last time.** With no race day open, the card on **Next Steps** starts tonight with the last race day's kind of night, classes and settings. **Set Up Tonight** is still there to change something first.
    - **The heats build themselves when entries close.** With **Entries close at:** set, the heats are built as it passes, ready to post. Untick **Build the heats when entries close** on **Setup** to build them yourself.
    - **Checked in late.** A driver who checks in after the heats are built goes into them by themselves before the heats are posted. After posting, **Next Steps** asks first, with **Add them to the heats...**. Either way nobody else moves. See [Checked in late](race-day/program-and-mains.md#checked-in-late).
    - **A late entry goes in by pace.** **Add a Late Entry** now picks the heat nearest the driver's pace that has room, not just the smallest. See [A late entry](race-day/program-and-mains.md#a-late-entry).

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Marshals in place, and the class running order (12 November).**
    - **Marshals in place.** Tick **Wait for the marshals before starting** on the **Auto start** switch's right-click menu and a race with marshals waits at zero until somebody presses **Marshals in place**. A minute before, a banner names the marshals and their posts. If they're not confirmed in time, the announcer calls them once. **Someone's missing...** logs a no-show, and the event report lists them. Off to start with. See [Marshals in place](race-day/program-and-mains.md#marshals-in-place).
    - **Class running order.** **Order...** on **Race Day → Setup** sets which class goes first in each round, and can turn the order round each round. A new race day keeps it. See [Race Day → Setup](race-day/events-and-entries.md#building-the-program).

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Less to set up each week (12 November).** A few quick wins for the race director:
    - **A new race day keeps last week's settings.** Besides the venue, layout, championship and heat sizes, it now keeps the entry fees and, from the last day of the same kind, **First race at:**, the first-round seeding, sharing heats, the between-race talk and the check-in rule. See [New Race Day](race-day/events-and-entries.md#new-race-day).
    - **Change a driver's classes at the desk.** Click their **Class** on **Who's Racing** to tick the classes they race tonight. The column now shows every class a driver races. See [Who's Racing](race-day/events-and-entries.md#race-day-whos-racing).
    - **The phone server starts by itself next time.** The first time **Start Server** works on **Local Screens**, **Start the server automatically when the app opens** is ticked for you, once. See [Phone and web](screens/phone-and-web.md).
    - **New classes have a race length.** While the app default has no lap or time limit, a new class starts at 5 minutes, so a first night's heat doesn't run until somebody finds **Checkered**.
    - **A new install is offered Set Up Tonight.** The empty race day a new install opens no longer stops the one-time "Would you like a hand setting up a race night?" offer.
    - **Guide fixes.** The sanctioning presets warning no longer says HeatCaller can't run multi-leg A-mains, and the first-race-night steps mention the race day a new install opens.

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Setting up the night: entries close, posting the heats, cars not heard and planned breaks (12 November).** Four additions for the race director, all on **Race Day → Setup**:
    - **Entries close at:** puts the club's cutoff on the day. The announcer calls it ten minutes before and as it passes, **Next Steps** counts down to it, and **Who's Racing** says when a check-in is a late entry. See [Race Day → Setup](race-day/events-and-entries.md#about-the-day).
    - **Post the heats.** Once the heats are built, **Next Steps** offers **Post the heats...** before the first race. It prints every class's heat sheets and the marshal list together and tells the room. After that, **Move Drivers Between Heats** and **Redo Remaining Heats** ask before changing a posted heat. Untick **Post the heats before the first race** to skip it. See [Post the heats](race-day/program-and-mains.md#post-the-heats).
    - **Cars not heard tonight.** The card and the posting window list any car on tonight's heats that hasn't crossed the loop tonight, practice included, so a dead transponder is found before Q1.
    - **Breaks...** plans breaks into the night, such as "water the track after Q2, 10 minutes". The finish time, the estimated starts and Auto start's gap allow for them, and the announcer says when racing starts again. See [Planned breaks](race-day/program-and-mains.md#planned-breaks).

  A new race day at the same venue keeps the entries-close time and the breaks. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **A closing checklist to tick off (11 November).** Once everything has run, **Next Steps** offers **Close the night...**: a window listing what's left (rentals, entries owed, results not on the club website, night emails, a backup, Close Day) with a button beside each that goes to the screen that sorts it, and **Back Up Now** for the backup. Lines tick themselves as the app sees each job done, and you can tick off anything you've dealt with another way. **Your Own Lines...** adds your club's own jobs, such as locking up. It's optional: untick **Close the night with a checklist** on **Race Day → Setup** to keep the single "Before lights out" line. See [The closing checklist](race-day/program-and-mains.md#the-closing-checklist). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Hand scoring: add a car the decoder never saw (11 November).** If the loop died before a car crossed the line, **Add a Car...** on the hand-scoring grid (and a list on the race-day manager's grid) puts it on the board with no laps, from this heat's roster or any known transponder, so its laps can be tapped. Undo takes it off while it has no laps, and if the decoder comes back the car is timed as usual. See [When the decoder goes down mid-race](race-control/corrections-and-recovery.md#when-the-decoder-goes-down-mid-race). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Estimated start times (11 November).** The printed and saved heat sheet shows when each round still to run should start, for example "about 20:42", and the phones' **Schedule** page shows the same for every race to come, updated as the night runs. They come from the same plan as the night's finish time on **Next Steps**. See [Heat sheets](race-day/program-and-mains.md#heat-sheets) and [On the Schedule page](online/index.md#on-the-schedule-page). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; the Schedule page **needs the relay redeployed** (`fly deploy --ha=false`).
- **The protest window reaches the phones and the announcer (11 November).** **Protest window (min):** has moved from **Race Day → Setup** to the **Club Website** page, under **Let drivers query a result**; a race day that had its own value hands it over. Once a class's last qualifier is in, the announcer says results are provisional and queries go to the desk, the **Schedule** page's qualifying shows "Provisional until 20:42", and when the window closes **Something wrong?** goes from that class's heats on drivers' phones. See [The protest window](race-day/program-and-mains.md#the-protest-window). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; the phone parts **need the relay redeployed** (`fly deploy --ha=false`).
- **A-Main overall said out loud, and bump-ups into every leg (11 November).** After the last leg of a double or triple A-Main, the announcer names the overall winner ("Sam wins the A-Main overall, best two of three legs") and the recap mentions it. Bump-ups from the B-Main now go into every leg still to run in one step, with one Undo. See [Double and triple A-mains](race-day/program-and-mains.md#double-and-triple-a-mains). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **The cold open is now the Tonight Open, and On Air plays it (11 November).** It no longer fires by itself in the first quiet gap of the evening. The first time you go **On Air** in a night, the Tonight Open plays before the jingle, with its own music and recording, and hands straight on to the show open. Its tick box on **Voice → AI Commentary**, its recording and its Big Screen caption are now called **Tonight Open**. A race started with **Stage**, or a demo, doesn't play it. See [The top and tail of the night](announcer/commentary.md#the-top-and-tail-of-the-night).
- **Import tonight's entries, and switching from LiveTime (11 November).** New **Import Entries** on **Race Day → Who's Racing** brings in an entry list from a CSV file (an RCSignup or LiveTime export, or any spreadsheet) or a copied table (a LiveRC entry list). Each row is matched to a driver already here by transponder, or by name when it has none; anyone new gets a profile; the row's class is added to the classes they race without changing their main class; and everyone on the list can be checked in to tonight's race day. Before anything changes, a preview lists every row: new, updated and how, already here, or skipped and why. A detail already on a profile is never replaced unless you tick **Replace details that differ**, and the whole import is one Undo. A new guide, [Switching from LiveTime](getting-started/switching-from-livetime.md), maps LiveTime's screens to HeatCaller's. See [Import entries](race-day/events-and-entries.md#import-entries). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Decoder-down mode (11 November).** If the decoder or the loop stops working mid-race, a **Score by Hand** button appears on the Next Up bar with the timing warning (or right-click a driver and choose **Score the race by hand (decoder down)...**). It opens a full-screen grid with one big button per car: tap a car each time it crosses the line, right-click or **Undo last tap** to take one back. The race-day manager's **Race** tab has the same grid on a phone or tablet. Each tap is a **+1 Lap**, so the laps show as counted by hand ("12*") in the result, and the announcer stops calling laps from the loop until the race ends. Afterwards, **Edit Result** has a new **Laps** column, so you can type a whole result's lap counts in one go. See [When the decoder goes down mid-race](race-control/corrections-and-recovery.md#when-the-decoder-goes-down-mid-race). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **"Leader behind" and race lines for drivers (11 November).** Two additions on drivers' phones:
    - **Leader behind you.** When the leader is about to lap a car, that driver's **Lap times in my ear** says "Leader behind" first in the lap's call, once each time the leader comes up to lap them, and never late. It's a new **What to say** box, on until unticked. See [Lap times in my ear](online/index.md#lap-times-in-my-ear).
    - **Race lines.** A race card on a driver's night page and **Me** can carry a line or two of their own, such as "Held off Sam for 4 laps", "Best lap 0.31s quicker than your Heat 1" or "Best Stock finish this year". The same lines go in their night email under "RACE BY RACE". See [Race cards](online/index.md#race-cards).

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC. **Both need the relay redeployed** (`fly deploy --ha=false`). "Leader behind" is said in the HeatCaller lap voice once its clips are made again (only the one new clip is made); until then that call is in the phone's voice.

- **A standby PC, and racing offline said out loud (11 November).** Two additions:
    - **Standby PC.** A second laptop on the track's network can keep a copy of the timing laptop's race data, fetched every 15 seconds, and **Take Over...** by hand if the timing laptop dies: it restores the copy, restarts, and you plug the decoder in. The copy is one way, only to PCs on the track's own network, guarded by a **Standby PIN** of 8 or more letters or digits, and never carries keys or PINs. Set it up on the new **Standby PC** card on **Backup & Restore**, on both laptops. There's no automatic failover. See [Standby PC](admin/standby-pc.md).
    - **"Online pages will catch up."** When the club website can't be reached, the **Next Up** bar on Race Control says "Website offline - racing carries on. Online pages will catch up", with how many updates are waiting, and Mission Control's **Website** tile says the same. See [When the internet drops](online/index.md#when-the-internet-drops).

  Update HeatCaller on both PCs; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Mixed-class heats (11 November).** Small classes can share a heat (3 Stock and 4 Mod as one race) and still be scored separately: each class keeps its own results, qualifying, mains, championship points and recap. Set **Share a heat under (drivers):** on **Race Day → Setup** to share them when you build, or select two heats and choose **Run Together...** on the program's right-click menu (with **Undo**). Shared heats show "Stock + Mod" on the program, heat sheet, Timetable and Big Screen, the announcer names each class's winner at the flag, and the curfew planner can suggest running two small classes together. See [Mixed-class heats](race-day/program-and-mains.md#mixed-class-heats). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **More qualifying and mains formats (11 November).** Three additions:
    - **Points per round.** A third **Qualifying:** choice in the class settings, the ROAR "Qual-Points" style. Each round ranks everyone across its heats on laps then time; 1st scores 1 point, 2nd 2, and so on; a missed or DQ'd round scores the field plus one. **Heats that count:** picks the best rounds, and fewest points wins. Ties go to the best single-round finish, then the best heat. The standings, the projection, the mains preview, the event report and drivers' phones all show the points, for example "6 pts (best 2 of 3)". See [Points per round](race-day/program-and-mains.md#points-per-round).
    - **Heads-up qualifying (HRQF).** New **First round seeded:** choice on **Race Day → Setup**. It seeds from tonight's practice (best 3 laps in a row), puts every other seed in each heat, reverses the grids in round 2, and reseeds round 3 on points when you build again after round 2. See [Heads-up qualifying](race-day/program-and-mains.md#heads-up-qualifying).
    - **Double and triple A-mains.** New **A-main:** setting in the class settings. The A-Main is built as legs ("A-Main Leg 1", "Leg 2"...), scored on points over the best legs, and the event report shows the **A-Main overall**. See [Double and triple A-mains](race-day/program-and-mains.md#double-and-triple-a-mains).

    Update HeatCaller on the race PC. Phones show the points after the relay is deployed; until then they show laps and time.

- **Marshal swaps (11 November).** **Swap...** in the **Corner Marshals** window covers a driver's post for one round with somebody else, and the marshal call, the Next Up bar and drivers' phones follow it. See [Corner marshals](race-day/program-and-mains.md#corner-marshals).
- **Money at the desk (11 November).** New **Entry fee:** and **Each extra class:** on **Race Day → Setup**. With a fee set, **Who's Racing** shows a **Paid** column (what each driver owes), and right-clicking marks drivers **Paid Cash**, **Paid Card**, **Comp** (marshal, volunteer, junior) or **Still Owing**, with Undo. The event report gets a **Money at the desk** section (cash, card, comps, and who still owes), and the closing list on Next Steps names drivers who still owe. HeatCaller only records it; it never takes a payment. See [Money at the desk](race-day/events-and-entries.md#money-at-the-desk). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Fair results and quick mains (11 November).** Four additions:
    - **The first round by recent pace.** Q1 now puts similar pace together, from each driver's best lap in the class over the last 60 days: the fastest last, and drivers with no lap yet with the slowest. A class nobody has a lap in is dealt as before. **First round seeded:** on **Race Day → Setup** also offers **Alphabetical** (as before) and **Random**. See [Build the qualifying program](race-day/program-and-mains.md#build-the-qualifying-program).
    - **The protest window.** A class's results stay provisional for 5 minutes after its last qualifier (**Protest window (min):** on **Setup**; 0 for none). The next-step card says until when, and building the finals before then asks first. See [The protest window](race-day/program-and-mains.md#the-protest-window).
    - **A mains preview.** **Build the finals from qualifying** now lists every driver under their main, with the qualifying result that put them there and the bump-up places marked, instead of only how many go in each.
    - **Add a Late Entry...** On the program's right-click menu: a driver who has just arrived goes into the smallest heat of each round still to run, and nobody else moves. See [A late entry](race-day/program-and-mains.md#a-late-entry).

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Auto start between races (11 November).** After each race on the program, Race Control counts down a gap (2:30 unless you choose another) and starts the next race by itself, through On Air as **Start Next** does. It's on by default; the new **Auto start** switch at the foot of the leaderboard turns it off for tonight. Some details:
    - The Next Up bar says "starts in 1:42", and **Hold** beside **Start Next** holds and resumes the count (right-click it for **+1 min** and **Start now**).
    - The call-ups (and the spoken countdown, when it's on) are timed to the real start, and phones show it.
    - A driver who raced the race just run gets an extra 2 minutes.
    - If a car in the next race hasn't been heard by the loop when the count runs out, the race waits for you: a banner offers **Start without them** or **Wait**, and the announcer asks the driver once to get the car on the track.

    - **The Big Screen** shows "NEXT RACE IN 1:42" in its countdown band between races.
    - **The phone remote** has **HOLD START** and **+1 MIN**.
    - **Heats built in rest-friendly order:** within a round, a driver in two classes gets a race off between their races wherever the heats allow.
    - **More time, or skip my race:** while their race is next up, a driver's **Me** page has **Need 5 more minutes** and **Car broken - skip me**. Approved at the desk, the race goes back 5 minutes, or they come out of it (with Undo). See [More time, or skip my race](online/driver-requests.md#more-time-or-skip-my-race).
    - **"Your race is in about 5 minutes"**: a new phone alert, once per race.

  See [Auto start between races](race-control/running-a-race.md#auto-start-between-races). Update HeatCaller on the race PC. **More time, skip my race and the 5-minute alert need the relay redeployed** (`fly deploy --ha=false`); the rest doesn't.

- **Plan the night (11 November).** Four additions:
    - **When the night finishes.** While races are still to run, the next-step card on **Next Steps** says when the night should finish, worked out from each race's length and tonight's own gap between races. **Tonight's program** gets an **About** column with when each race should start. See [When the night finishes](race-day/program-and-mains.md#when-the-night-finishes).
    - **Out of the venue by.** A new **Out of the venue by:** time on **Race Day → Setup**. With it set, the card says whether the night finishes in time and, when it won't, by how much and what would help, biggest saving first: drop the last qualifying round, run qualifiers a minute shorter, or run one main fewer. A new race day takes the time from the last day at the same venue.
    - **We're Done After This Round...** On the program's right-click menu: finishes the qualifying round under way, drops every later one that hasn't run (with Undo), and the announcer tells the room. See [We're done after this round](race-day/program-and-mains.md#were-done-after-this-round).
    - **Closing the night.** Once everything has run, the card lists what's left before lights out: rentals still out, results not on the club website, night emails to send, a backup of tonight's results, and Close Day. See [Closing the night](race-day/program-and-mains.md#closing-the-night).

  Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Night pace (11 November).** The event report has a new **Night pace** section. It shows how far apart the races started, the usual time from a finish to the next green, the share of the night on track, races run against the program, and the three longest gaps with what HeatCaller saw happen in each: building heats, building the finals, correcting a result, or waiting at the grid. When every round has been run, the same numbers appear on one line on the race program's next-step card. Races now save the time they were staged. See [Night pace](race-control/results-and-championships.md). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **A drag handle for Tonight's program (11 November).** On **Race Day → Next Steps**, **Tonight's program** has a handle across its top, like the one under the list on **Race Calls**. Drag it up or down and the program and **Next Steps** resize as you drag, instead of after you let go. Double-click it to put it back. See [Program and mains](race-day/program-and-mains.md).
- **Hold Start Next for a demo run (11 November).** On **Race Control**, pressing and holding **Start Next** on the Next Up bar for about a second runs the next round as a demo, with its own drivers, format and show. A bar fills along the button while you hold, and its tooltip says "Hold for a demo run". A normal click still starts the race for real. The demo keeps every demo rule: it never scores and is never filed as the round's real result. The hold does nothing while a race is running. See [The Next Up bar](race-control/running-a-race.md#the-next-up-bar).
- **HeatCaller moves to ElevenLabs v4 Turbo (10 November).** Every ElevenLabs voice now uses `eleven_v4_turbo`, including voices set to another model, and **Race calls:** goes back to the same model, since v4 Turbo is quick enough for race calls. The HeatCaller voice on the relay and the relay's spoken lap times move to it too. v4 plays audio tags much harder than v3, so the built-in lines were retagged: `[shouting]` is kept for the start and finish, a new leader, records and the flags, `[excited]` for passes and the last lap, and the routine calls are read plain. **This replaces every call's lines with the new built-in ones, including lines you wrote yourself**, though which calls are on and their weights stay as they were. A built-in soundscape you applied keeps its own lines. On v4 the AI commentary tags only the first sentence of a recap. See [Audio tags](announcer/voice.md#audio-tags).
- **A driver's own bests are no longer excited (11 November).** Personal bests, a best ever here, several personal bests said together, streaks, qualifying improvements and achievements lose `[excited]` and `[shouting]`; their `[cheerful]` and `[awe]` stay. Those two tags are taken out of these calls' lines, including lines you wrote; every other line stays as it is. See [Audio tags](announcer/voice.md#audio-tags).

## 0.62.0

Released 9 Nov 2026. Everything since 0.61.0, newest first.

- **The position report is corrected when the order changes (9 November).** If a car it names changes place while the report is being read out, the report stops after a beat and a short correction is said next, with the new order by places: "Hold on - Sam takes the lead! So it's Sam leads, Bill second, Frank third." Before, the report always finished, and the next one could be several laps away. A car crossing the line without changing place doesn't stop it. For a change of lead the correction is the lead change call, so it isn't said twice. A penalty or another correction by hand gets "And with that ruling…". A report dropped before it starts, because the order moved while it was being prepared, is now queued once more, worded from the board as it stands then, so you aren't left waiting laps for the next one. See [On a short track](announcer/voice.md#on-a-short-track). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Short tracks are called out (9 November).** The announcer is least accurate where laps average under 8 seconds: calls are skipped, can be a lap old when heard, and the commentary can mix up close lap times. Timing and scoring aren't affected. The user guide says so under [On a short track](announcer/voice.md#on-a-short-track), and so do the beta terms, so HeatCaller asks you to accept them again the next time it opens. HeatCaller also warns you: an amber line under the **Session** card on **Race Control** while the track picked is a short one, judged by its real laps once 10 have been raced, or the average typed on **Track Layouts**, where the same warning shows as you type it.
- **Three character tones for the commentary (8 November).** **Writing tone:** on **AI Commentary** adds **Villain**, **Pirate** and **Town crier**. Each one changes the words of every line, the recap, the segments between races and the pit-lane interviews. Each is still told to keep every name, position and number exactly as given, and to keep it family-friendly. With an ElevenLabs v3 voice, each tone has its own audio tags (a laugh, a shout) under **Give the lines their tone's delivery when they're spoken**, which was **Mark the lines excited**. See [Character tones](announcer/commentary.md#character-tones).
- **A Desk button on Race Control (7 November).** While drivers' requests from their phones are waiting, a cyan **Desk** button appears on the flag row with a red badge counting them. Click it to open **Driver Requests**. It goes when none are waiting, and the row re-spaces itself as it comes and goes. See [Running a race](race-control/running-a-race.md).
- **The achievements reader gets the AI too (7 November).** With commentary on, the AI writes what the achievements reader says, as it does for the Technical Lead and the Team Principal. It's given what each achievement is for, how rare it is at the club, and whether it's a driver's first, and it leads with the biggest one. HeatCaller checks that each achievement goes to the driver who earned it and that nobody is left out. A read that fails the check goes out as HeatCaller's own read instead, as does one that isn't ready in time. See [The achievements read](announcer/commentary.md#the-achievements-read).
- **The commentary hears what race control did (7 November).** Everything here was already saved and never handed to the AI commentary.
    - **The race recap** now knows each warning and its reason, jump starts, penalties, black flags, DNFs and DQs, corrections and their reasons, and the race director's note. When a penalty changed the winner, it says who had it on the road. For a class scored on handicap it says who won on handicap, and it mentions a driver who beat the target lap race control set them. See [What race control did](announcer/commentary.md#what-race-control-did).
    - **Tonight's recap** picks up corrections made after a race's own recap (what changed, not the reason typed for it), and the race director's notes. The sign-off doesn't.
    - **Championship lines** say when a driver's rounds were dropped under the best-rounds rule, their carried-in points, and rounds without a lap. See [What is behind a total](announcer/commentary.md#what-is-behind-a-total).
    - **The form guide** says where each driver stands in the championship, and a driver who has **missed just one** of the club's last six nights gets a line too.
    - **Driver introductions** can add one more thing from a driver's time here: practice laps this week, laps led in races here, days on track, or tonight's target lap.
    - **A season's piece.** **A season:** on **Club Website → Recording** writes one championship's whole season and plays it, for a prize night. See [A season's piece](announcer/commentary.md#a-seasons-piece).

## 0.61.0

Released 7 Nov 2026. Everything since 0.60.0, newest first.

- **The known issues, again (7 November).**
    - **Record in danger**, a new **During the race** segment on **Voice → Commentary**: the announcer calls a driver whose last few laps are closing on the layout record, before it falls. Until now this was only written to the console. See [Commentary](announcer/commentary.md).
    - **"Nobody can catch him now" after the flag.** With **Rounds in the season** filled in, **Championship check** says "That settles it: nobody can catch Bill in Box Stock now", or how many drivers can still win, proved the same way the race intro proves it. See [What the championship still has riding on it](announcer/commentary.md#what-the-championship-still-has-riding-on-it).
    - **Announcer's note** on a championship's **Scoring Rules**: one sentence in your own words, read out as typed at the cold open and the sign-off. See [Championships](race-control/results-and-championships.md).
    - **Visiting from another club** on a driver's profile: the cold open counts them ("7 of them visiting"). See [Drivers](race-day/drivers-and-classes.md).
    - **The club's live board shows laps counted by hand** ("12\*") with the line "\* includes laps counted by hand", as the laptop's own pages do. Update HeatCaller on the race PC and redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`).
    - **Re-roll Line** refuses a line saved with full names before **Shorten published names** was ticked, so the full names aren't sent to the online voice again. Use **Forget Line** for those.

- **The help catches up with 3-6 November (6 November).** Every page of the guide was checked against the app and the relay again, and the screenshots were retaken. What it now covers, and hadn't:
    - **Kokoro stand-ins for the whole cast.** On **Voice → Cast**, each speaker has an **If ElevenLabs runs out:** voice with its own **Test Voice**. When your ElevenLabs allowance runs out, or the usage chip reaches 100%, each speaker switches to their own Kokoro voice and saved ElevenLabs lines are held back, so the night stays in one set of voices. See [Each of the cast when ElevenLabs runs out](announcer/voice.md#each-of-the-cast-when-elevenlabs-runs-out).
    - **Race Control:** the level meters sit under **Broadcast** at the foot of the clock column (see [Level meters](race-control/running-a-race.md#level-meters)), and a lap breakdown scrolls back through every lap.
    - **Manage Navigation** shows in Simple mode too.
    - **Retire** asks "Are you sure?" first, for transponders, packs, cars and chassis types.
    - **Choose Live Board Columns** on **Club Website** picks the columns of the relay's live board. See [Live board columns](online/setup.md#live-board-columns).
    - **The relay's pages:** the new Home, the Live page's flag card, charts and track map, race grids and running late on Schedule, head to head, club health and theoretical best on Results, the Tonight, Races, Garage and Stats spaces on **Me**, a driver's garage from their phone, and push alerts. See [Your club's pages](online/index.md#your-clubs-pages), [Alerts](online/index.md#alerts), [Garage](online/index.md#garage) and [A driver's garage from their phone](race-day/drivers-and-classes.md#a-drivers-garage-from-their-phone). Hosting them is in [Push alerts](admin/relay-hosting.md#push-alerts).

- **The standard first-night guide is club rules (6 November).** **Your first night - what happens** now covers checking in, safety in the pits, batteries, the driver stand, racing clean, the start, marshalling, race control and leaving the place tidy, instead of how the page works. A club's own guide is unchanged. A club that had sent the old standard guide unchanged gets the new one; the **Club Website** page shows it in the box. Update HeatCaller on the race PC and redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`).

- **The first-night guide on Home (6 November).** **Your first night - what happens** is now the first thing on Home, for every phone, folded until tapped. **Got it - hide this** hides it for good on that phone, here and on the check-in and night pages.

- **A shorter Home (6 November).** The **You** card (a remembered driver's best finish, standings place and newest achievement) is gone, since **Me** has all of it. So is the **Up next** card at the top while the next race is only waiting: **On track now** still shows while a race is running, and **Schedule** has the running order.

- **A shorter Your details card on Me (6 November).** It now lists what's on file for the driver, a line each, with an **Ask the desk to change** button. The form opens from the bottom of the screen instead of unfolding inside the card, so the card is about a third as tall. See [Your details](online/driver-requests.md#your-details).

- **Your details on Tonight (6 November).** On **Me**, **Your details** (asking the desk to change how you are called and shown) is now at the bottom of **Tonight**, under **Right now**, instead of in **Garage**.

- **Me starts higher up (6 November).** **Signed in** sits on the same line as the driver's name instead of a row of its own above it, and the card under the name is closer to it, so everything on **Me** starts nearer the top.

- **No status strip on Me (6 November).** The row of chips under the driver's name (their next race, the pack in the car, the transponder) is gone: the card under it says the same. The **Tonight**, **Races**, **Garage** and **Stats** switch now sticks to the very top as you scroll. Redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`).

- **Every lap stat at once (6 November).** On a driver's night, each race's **Lap stats** shows **Avg. top 5**, **Avg. top 10**, **Avg. top 15**, **Top 2 consec.** and **Std. deviation** straight under the first four. The **More stats** button is gone. Redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`).

- **A shorter Right now card on Me (6 November).** The **Check in** button and the pack charge timer are gone from the **Right now** card. The top of **Me** still offers **Check in** until the driver is checked in, and signing in on **Me** with a personal PIN checks them in too. Home's **Your next race** no longer shows **On charge**. Redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`).

- **Signing out of Me asks first, and forgets everything (6 November).** A driver's night on the relay shows **Signed in** at the top once the phone remembers them. Tapping it asks "Sign out on this phone?". **Sign out** forgets the driver and everything Me kept on that phone for them, including their PIN sign-in, and **Me** asks for a personal PIN again. **Back** and reloading go to the PIN too. **Sign out on this phone** at the bottom asks the same question. See [Getting around](online/index.md#getting-around). Redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`).

- **Relay page changes (4 November).**
    - **Make a picture to share** on a driver's night does something on a phone now. It used to save the picture as a download, which a phone quietly drops, always from the home-screen icon. Now it opens the phone's share sheet with the picture in it. Where the browser can't share a picture, the picture opens on the page to press and hold, with a **Save picture** button.
    - **Save as a text file** is gone from the night page. The picture is the way to share a night.
    - On **Live**, the announcer's words beside **Listen live** are three times the size, in white and bold. With listening off it says "Press Listen Live to hear the broadcast."
    - The **Captions** card under **Message the announcer** is gone when the club has listening on, since **Listen live** already shows the words. A club without listening still gets it.

    Redeploy the relay; nothing changes on the race PC.

- **How chatty is the one dial for the AI commentary (3 November).** Turned all the way up, the AI could still go quiet in the heats. Each segment kept its own limits for the night and per class, and its own gap after the last line, and **How chatty** reached none of them. Now it moves them all. **Non-stop** lifts the nightly and per-class limits, shortens the gaps to about a third, and saves nothing for the mains. **Lively** doubles the limits; **Sparing** and **Measured** lower them. **Standard** is unchanged, and a once-a-night segment like the cold open stays once a night at every stop. **Pacing**, which only ever spaced the built-in timing calls, moved to **Voice > Announcements > How often**. **Segments run** is gone: any segments your choice there turned off are now unticked instead, so nothing you hear changes. Each demo run now gets a night of its own, so rehearsals no longer use up the real heats' segments. The **Lines not said this race** window on Race Control opens with a line on what the AI said and what held the rest. See [How chatty](announcer/commentary.md#how-chatty). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Recap and battle watch fixes (3 November).**
    - A battle watch thrown away because its two cars swapped before a word was said no longer counts toward its limits for the night and the class. Before, a close fight could use up its own limit in silence.
    - The race recap no longer shouts: words written in capitals ("WELCOME BACK", "FIVE!") are put back into normal case, as the race intros already were, with names kept as spelled. A voice that isn't v3 read them as letters.
    - Times the AI spells out one digit at a time ("three-point-three-zero-four", "a twelve-nine-four-five") are said like the rest, as "three and a half seconds back" or "a twelve ninety-four", and the AI is asked not to write them that way.
    - The **Cold Open** ("Welcome to the track, everybody!") no longer plays inside the recap show, between the recap's parts.
    - The sign-off no longer names the race that just finished as next. After a demo it names the round after the demo's. A sign-off that still gets it wrong is replaced by the show's own.

    Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **More calls stop when the order changes under them (3 November).** Until now, only a **Battle watch** line stopped when its two cars swapped. These calls now do too:
    - **What's still to decide** ("Sam is taking three tenths a lap out of Bill"): if Sam gets past while it's being said, it stops and the pass is called, the same as a battle line.
    - **Close battle**: the same. If the two cars swap before the line has been said, it's dropped and the pass is called.
    - **Leader change**: if Bill takes the lead straight back before "Sam takes the lead" is said, the line stops and you hear the new lead change instead.
    - **Rivalries**: if the rival gets straight back past, the line stops and the rivalry line for that swap is said instead.

    A call that was waiting for the end of another line, and whose two cars swap while it waits, is dropped instead of being said late. The position report isn't cut: it's already re-read from the board just before it's said, and once it has started it finishes. The next report covers anything that changed. See [Announcements](announcer/voice.md#announcements). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Listen live hears the whole room (2 November).** Listening along on the club's **Live** page used to play only the announcer's lines, one after another, in silence. Now it plays everything the room hears: the announcer, the show music, the crowd, the stingers and beds, and the tones, as one continuous stream a few seconds behind. The button is now called **Listen live**. Lines that never went to listeners still don't: penalty calls, and lines naming a driver who is shown by initials online, are taken out of the stream while the music carries on under them. If the page goes to the background, for example when you switch to another app on an iPhone, coming back no longer plays everything you missed, a long way behind: it drops it and picks up the room where it is now. Update HeatCaller on the race PC and redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`). A race PC that hasn't been updated still sends the announcer's lines, and the new page still plays them.

- **Small fixes found checking the user guide (2 November).**
    - **Save Support Info** now includes the Beta decoders' raw capture files (`decoder-raw-*.log`), which their console message asks you to send.
    - <kbd>F1</kbd> in the **Broadcast** window or a lap breakdown window no longer skips On Air when it's already running: like the main window, it says to hold <kbd>0</kbd>.
    - The welcome wizard's Timing step no longer tells you to plug in an EasyLap when a Beta decoder is picked.
    - **Test** on **Voice → Race Calls** fills in every placeholder, and never reads one out in braces.
    - A setup whose chassis sheet was deleted shows "(sheet deleted)" instead of a blank sheet.
    - **Share Results Card** no longer shows a **Size:** list that did nothing and changed the charts' saved size.
    - **Pace** no longer lists pasted results, which have no lap times.
    - In Simple mode, double-clicking one of your own looks no longer opens the editor, and Themes has the **More settings in Advanced mode** line.
    - Messages that named old places now name the current ones: the Broadcast window for Notes and Votes, **Race Day → Setup** to reopen a day, **Voice → Race Calls**, and the **Who writes it** card.

    Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Notes and votes from the club's own page (2 November).** **Take them from the club's own web page** and **Let the club's own web page vote**, on the **Room & Web** card, did nothing anybody could see: the relay could take a note or a vote, but none of the club's pages offered a way to send one. Now the club's **Live** page has a **Message the announcer** card while the notes switch is on, and a **Who wins?** card while the vote switch is on and a vote is open. They work like the `/shoutout` and `/vote` pages on the club's Wi-Fi: same words, same limits, every note still waits on Race Control for your approval, and no tally is shown. See [Room & Web](online/setup.md#room-web). Redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`); nothing changes in HeatCaller on the race PC.
- **"Backup & Restore" in the sidebar (2 November).** The sidebar showed the page as "Backup _Restore": the "&" was read as a keyboard-shortcut marker. It now reads **Backup & Restore**. The user guide also has screenshots now, on most pages. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Five more decoders, in Beta (1 November).** **Connect via:** now also offers **MyLaps / AMB P3 (Beta)** (AMBrc, RC4 and others, over the network or a COM port), **ZRound open protocol / RiCino (Beta)**, **Cano / RCHourglass (Beta)**, **OpenStint (Beta)** and **EasyLap / Robitronic Serial (Beta)**. A new **Source:** row picks a COM port, the network or a simulated decoder, and each decoder remembers its own settings. They're written from each decoder's published format and haven't yet been tested on a real one, so they stay marked Beta until they have, and the console says so each time one connects. Run your usual timing alongside the first time, and keep the raw capture file. The simulated source plays a decoder of that type with your own drivers' transponders, so you can rehearse with it at home. They can be the line but not a split loop yet. See [The Beta decoders](timing/decoders.md#the-beta-decoders). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.

- **Simple mode, the last touches (1 November).** The welcome wizard's last step now says HeatCaller starts in Simple mode and where to turn on Advanced mode, and the app tour has a stop on the **Settings mode** card. For a PC volunteers run, **Ask for the manager PIN to turn on Advanced mode** on that card (in Advanced mode, once a manager PIN is set on **Local Screens**) asks for the **Manager PIN** before the warning. It does nothing while no manager PIN is set, so you can't lock yourself out, and a restore leaves it alone. The help has a new page, [Simple and Advanced mode](getting-started/simple-and-advanced-mode.md). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **The race's fastest lap is always said (1 November).** The fastest-lap call was thrown away whenever another line was playing, so the last one heard was often not the lap on the board. It now waits its turn, like the clock calls, and a newer fastest lap replaces one still waiting, whoever set it, so only the current holder is ever said. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Gap to P1 to the thousandth (1 November).** The **Gap to P1** and **Interval** columns now show the gap to the thousandth ("+2.345", was "+2.35"), on Race Control, the Big Screen, the Wi-Fi pages and the club's pages, matching the lap times and the running order, which says the gap to the thousandth. The AI recap is given the winning margin to the thousandth too, and the Announcer screen's talking points show it that way. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Restore default settings (1 November).** The **Settings mode** card on **Backup & Restore** has two new buttons. **Restore Default Settings...** puts every setting Simple mode hides back to how HeatCaller ships and turns Simple mode back on. It keeps your drivers, classes, tracks, race days and results, sponsors, venues and garage, your keys, the club website's club, token and address, PINs and email sign-in, the decoder's connection and port, your backups, and the crowd your soundscape set. Tick **Also reset what Simple mode shows** to put the track, lap count, voice, volumes, look and commentary switches back too. Your settings are saved as a copy first, and **Put Back Earlier Settings...** lists those copies by date (the last 10 are kept) and puts back the one you pick. Either way HeatCaller restarts to use them, so neither is offered while a race or practice is running. Going back to Simple with hidden settings changed now offers **Restore Defaults...** too. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Simple mode finishes the job (1 November).** Typing "advanced", "simple mode" or "default settings" in the sidebar search finds Backup & Restore, where the Settings mode card is. A button or shortcut that would open a page Simple mode hides, such as **Venues**, now asks to turn on Advanced mode first. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Who writes it first on AI Commentary (1 November).** In Advanced mode, the **Who writes it** card is now the first card on **Voice → AI Commentary**, above **How it's written**. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **The running order is heard again (1 November).** With the position report switched on, it was hardly ever said. Since the 31 October update, a report that waited more than 3 seconds behind another line was dropped, and since the report started waiting for the last car it names to cross the line, it's queued at the very moment that car's own lap call, streak or fast lap goes in ahead of it, so it nearly always waited longer than that. Now it waits its turn however long that takes. Just before it's spoken the board is read again, so it still never reads out an order the screen has moved on from, and a newer report still replaces one that's waiting. The console no longer says "Dropped PositionReport - it waited...". See [Every announcement](announcer/voice.md#every-announcement). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Call Up pressed twice calls the race once (1 November).** The A-Main could be called up three times in fifteen seconds. **Call Up** (and <kbd>F12</kbd>, and **CALL UP** on the phone) says the call-up straight away whatever the Between races settings, but the voice takes a second or two to start, so a press looked like it had done nothing and the next press called the race again. Now a press while the last call-up is still waiting to be said, or within 30 seconds of it, is answered "Already called up A-Main 7s ago." on the phone and in the console, and nothing is said. A press after 30 seconds still calls the race up, as before. Every press of **Call Up** is now in the console too, so a call-up somebody pressed can be told from the automatic one. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **The first word of a call-up is heard in full (1 November).** The between-races call-ups ("Q2 Heat 1 for Stock is next. Drivers, to the stand please.") sounded clipped at the very start. Every line has a fixed slice cut off the front of its audio to get rid of a burst of static some voice files start with, and most lines start talking after that slice, so only silence went. A call-up opens on a hard word like "Q2" or "A-Main", and the voice starts saying it sooner, so the slice took the start of the word. Now the cut stops just before the voice starts, so a line that speaks straight away keeps its first word. Lines that start quietly are cut the same as before. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **A battle call stops when the pass happens (1 November).** A **Battle watch** line takes ten seconds or so to say, and a close fight can swap places in that time, so the announcer could finish "Sam is all over the back of Bill" with Sam already in front on the board. Now the line stops mid-sentence the moment the car it called "behind" gets ahead, and the announcer calls the pass straight away: "And Sam takes the lead!", or "And Sam is through, up into third!" for any other place. It only happens when those two cars swap: a third car going past both, or any other change in the order, leaves the line alone, and each pass is called once. For the lead, the pass call takes the place of the **Leader change** call for that crossing, so you don't hear both. A battle line that's still waiting its turn when its two cars swap is dropped without being said. See [AI Commentary](announcer/commentary.md). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **A yellow and orange sun on Classic (1 November).** With the **Classic** soundscape (it wears the **Synthwave** look), or **Synthwave** picked as the look, HeatCaller's small sun came out pink: by the club's name, on Live's **LIVE** badge and in the footer of the club's pages, in the footer of the pages on the club's Wi-Fi, and on the Big Screen beside the countdown. It took the look's main color, and Synthwave's is pink. On that look the sun is now a sunset from yellow to orange, like HeatCaller's logo, and only the horizon lines under it stay pink. Every other look keeps its sun in its own main color, as before. Update HeatCaller on the race PC and redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`).
- **Simple mode hides the tuning (1 November).** In Simple mode, the tabs, cards and rows that are tuning are now hidden, and Advanced mode shows them all again at once, each in its usual place, with no restart. Hidden isn't off: every hidden setting keeps its value and keeps working. A page with something hidden ends with a grey **More settings in Advanced mode** line, which opens the Advanced warning. Searching the sidebar for something hidden (try "ducking" or "split loop") lists **Advanced mode settings** under **IN ADVANCED MODE**. Going back to Simple with hidden settings changed from their defaults says so, once. What Simple hides:
    - **Sidebar:** the **Venues** page and the **Manage Navigation** button.
    - **Race Control:** **Start races On Air** and **Columns**. In **Race Format**, only the lap limit, time limit and start type show.
    - **Race Day:** which race **Start the next race automatically** reaches for (the tick itself stays).
    - **Championship:** **Scoring Rules...**. **Classes:** **Tech Checklist**, **Qualifier Format** and **Warnings** in the class editor.
    - **Practice:** **Rolling average over** and **Choose Columns...**.
    - **Themes:** **New Look**, **Edit** and **Animate the Synthwave sidebar header (scrolling grid, twinkling stars)**.
    - **Sponsors:** **Rotation timing** and **Video sound**.
    - **Local Screens:** **Port**, the **Race-day manager (phone)** card and the column choosers.
    - **Club Website:** the **Recording** tab, the relay **Address**, **Include photos**, and the **What gets published**, **Look**, **Personal PINs**, **Email**, **Race-day manager** and **Room & Web** cards.
    - **Voice:** the **Race Calls** tab, the ElevenLabs **Model**, **Voice parameters**, **Saved voice lines**, the voice relay rows and the consent rows. On **Cast**, the Recap host, Technical Lead, Team Principal and Achievements reader cards, and the co-host's **What they're like** and **How much they talk**.
    - **Sounds:** the **Imaging**, **Effects**, **Ambience** and **Lap Beeps** tabs (they still play as the soundscape sets them). On **Mixer**, the **Imaging levels**, **Ducking**, **When a session starts** and **When it ends** cards and **Reset to Defaults**. On **Soundscapes**, **Save Current Settings as New**, **Previews include the announcer's voice**, **Ask which sound to use in Set Up Tonight**, and each card's **Customize** and **Voice Lines**. On **Music**, the **Behavior** card.
    - **Connection:** **Run timing on its own thread**, **Record every pass to a file**, the split loop rows and the **Developer console**.
    - **AI Commentary** becomes **Enable AI commentary** and a **What it talks about** card with six switches: **Before the race**, **During the race**, **Between races**, **After the flag**, **Recap show** and **Opening and closing the night**, each over the pieces the tab already has. Off turns its pieces off and remembers which were on; on puts those back, or with nothing remembered, turns them on as HeatCaller ships them. A section you set piece by piece in Advanced shows **custom** beside it, and off and on again keeps your set. The pit lane throws, the booth interview and the achievements only come on if their voice is set on the Cast tab, so a switch never turns on something silent. **After the flag** shows **needs Recap show** when the recaps are off. **Talking points on the Announcer screen** sits under them. With **Enable AI commentary** off, the switches are grayed, not hidden, so you can see what you'd get.
    - Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Race calls on or off (1 November).** **Voice → Voice** has a new **Race calls:** row with **In races** and **In practice**. Untick one and every call on the **Race Calls** tab goes quiet there, while each call keeps its own ticks for when you turn it back on. Sound effects and the AI commentary aren't race calls and aren't affected. Both are on, so nothing changes until you untick one. Applying a soundscape leaves them as they are. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **A shorter hint under the flag row (1 November).** The line under Race Control's flag buttons now says "Red, Checkered and Reset: press and hold." instead of adding "for a second. Everything else is one click.", and the gap above the On Air bar is a little bigger. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Simple mode and Advanced mode, the switch (1 November).** HeatCaller now has two modes. **Simple mode** will show what a race night needs, and **Advanced mode** every setting, as the app is today. This first step adds the switch only: nothing is hidden yet, and the pages look the same in both modes. To turn Advanced mode on, go to **Backup & Restore**, find the new **Settings mode** card and press **Turn On Advanced Mode...**. A warning says what changing the tuning settings can break, and the button waits until you tick **I understand changes could break how HeatCaller works.** While Advanced mode is on, an amber **Advanced** sits at the bottom of the sidebar; click it, or **Back to Simple Mode** on the card, to go back. The mode is kept per PC, so the track PC can stay in Simple while your own stays in Advanced. A new install starts in Simple. An install updating to this version is put in Advanced if any setting that Simple will hide has been changed from how HeatCaller ships, so nothing you tuned disappears from view, and in Simple otherwise; a one-time note says which. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **The help brought up to date with the last few days (1 November).** The help pages now cover HeatCaller's mark on the app's outputs and the club's pages (and the sun as every club's home-screen icon), the position report's exact gaps and the new `{gaps}` placeholder with **Say each car's gap to the leader**, the lap windows' limits and menu, the split colors on the Big Screen, the Voice page's tab names (**Voice**, **Cast**, **Race Calls**, **AI Commentary**), Me remembering a driver, **Go to Me** after sign-up, and the license acceptance file. <kbd>F1</kbd> is no longer described as skipping On Air, and American spelling is used throughout. Nothing changes in the app or on the relay.
- **On Air is on <kbd>0</kbd> now, and skipping it is a hold (1 November).** <kbd>O</kbd> was easy to mix up with the Big Screen's own <kbd>O</kbd>, and one stray tap skipped the whole opening. Now a tap of <kbd>0</kbd> (top row or keypad) starts On Air, and while it runs you hold <kbd>0</kbd> for a second to skip to Stage, the same as holding **Red**, **Checkered** or **Reset**. The strip under the flag row fills while you hold and says "Let go too soon - nothing was done." if you let go early. The press that starts On Air never turns into the skip, however long you hold it. <kbd>F1</kbd> no longer skips On Air either; pressed while it runs, it does nothing. <kbd>O</kbd> does nothing on Race Control now, and still switches the ON AIR band on the Big Screen. The phone remote's **Stage** still skips On Air while it runs, as before. See [On Air](race-control/running-a-race.md#on-air). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Timed races are called in time, not laps (1 November).** In a race that was only timed, the announcer could say "four circuits to run". The AI commentary's **What's still to decide** line counted every race in laps, worked out from the clock, and the model said that count out loud. Now a race the clock ends is talked about in time ("about a minute and a half left", or "the final lap" once time's up), and only a race run to a lap count hears laps left. Every line the AI commentary writes during a race is also told whether the race is timed, run to a lap count, or both, so none of them counts laps in a timed race. In a race with a lap limit and a time limit, the time calls (**Final minute**, **Minutes left**, **Last minute countdown**) now stay quiet once the leader's pace shows the lap limit will end it first. See [Lap-limited and timed races](race-control/running-a-race.md#lap-limited-and-timed-races). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Each driver on the Big Screen as they're introduced (1 November).** During the driver introductions, the Big Screen now shows each driver as the announcer names them: their photo, name, grid spot, number and home club, sliding in with the voice and gone two seconds after the line ends, or at once if a flag cuts it off. The grid roll call comes back after. It's on by default; untick **Show each driver's name and photo on the Big Screen as they're introduced** on **Voice → AI Commentary** to turn it off. To keep a young driver's photo off it, tick **Keep their photo off it** on the **Big Screen:** row of their profile: they get their initials instead, and their name still shows. The profile's **ONLINE PRIVACY** group is now called **PRIVACY**. See [Driver introductions](screens/big-screen.md#driver-introductions). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **The running order waits for every car it names, and gives exact gaps (1 November).** The position report was read the moment the leader crossed the line, when every car behind was still a lap short, so each gap it gave was a lap old. Third place and on were also timed to the car just ahead of them, not to the leader like the board's **Gap to P1** column, and every gap was rounded ("a second and a half back", "right on their tail"). Now the report waits until the last car it names has crossed the line too, then reads the order and gives each car its gap to the leader exactly, to the thousandth: "Bill one point two three four back, Frank two point oh five back". "Right on their tail" and the other rounded wording are gone from the report. A car that never comes round holds it up for half a lap longer than the leader's last lap at most; the report is then read without that car and anyone behind it. Under **Read every** _n_ **lap(s) of the LEADER, down to P** _n_, a line now says when the report is read, and turns into a warning past P3, since a deeper report is heard well after the leader crosses. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Blue split times mean the quickest of anyone (1 November).** On the **S1** to **S4** columns, blue marks the quickest sector of the session and a driver's own best is green, but two drivers could both look blue. The Big Screen painted a driver's own best, a streak and **At Split** up in the same blue as the session's quickest, and in the **Synthwave** look the green was a cyan only a shade off the blue. Now the Big Screen paints a driver's own good news in the look's green, as Race Control does, and **Synthwave**'s green is a real green, the one its online pages already used. **British Racing Green** gets a pale brass accent and **Ice** an indigo one, for the same reason. A look you made yourself gets a new readability warning when its accent and good colors look alike. Two drivers on exactly the same sector time are both blue, since they share it. See [Split times](timing/decoders.md#split-times). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **<kbd>O</kbd> stops the announcer when it skips On Air (1 November).** Pressing <kbd>O</kbd> again while On Air was going out staged the race, but the announcer kept talking: the rest of the opening, the driver introductions and pit lane all played out, and only then the staging call. On Air hands each part to the announcer as it goes, and the skip stopped On Air adding more without taking back what it had already handed over, so the staging call waited behind all of it. Now <kbd>O</kbd> cuts the announcer off at once, the same way **Reset** does, and the staging call is the next thing heard. The same goes when **Stage**, **Countdown**, **Green** or **Reset** calls On Air off, and when a flag skips a demo's On Air. See [On Air](race-control/running-a-race.md#on-air). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **One bar that stays on a home-screen icon (1 November).** Opened from a home-screen icon on a phone, the club's pages used to swap the whole screen on every tap, bottom bar included, so the tab buttons flickered even with the page above them steady. Now the bar is drawn once and stays on screen, and only the page above it changes. The next page loads behind the one you're looking at and takes its place once it's ready, for the tabs and for links between the club's pages. The address still names the page you're on. In Safari and Chrome tabs nothing changes. Redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`); nothing changes on the race PC.
- **The running order names the right car second (1 November).** The position report is written the moment the leader crosses the line, then waits its turn behind the leader's own lap call. On a short lap the cars behind cross the line in that time, so a car that had just got past second place could be second on the screen while the voice still named the car it passed, and the time given for second place was a lap old. Now the report reads the board again just before it's spoken: if anyone in it has crossed the line since, or been given a penalty, it's said from the order as it stands, with the newest times, in the same line your club picked, and the Big Screen shows the new words. A report where nothing moved is said exactly as before. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Steady tabs on a phone (1 November).** Tapping a tab on the club's pages used to blank the screen, show **Loading...** and then pop the page in, and the tapped tab flashed grey. Now the tab lights the moment you tap it, never turns grey, and the next page opens already drawn with what your phone last saw there, then updates itself straight away. While your finger is on a tab, that page's latest data is already being fetched. Copies are kept only on the phone, for the open window, and are never more than ten minutes old (thirty seconds for the race state, and Live never reads out a lap from one). The first visit to a page still shows **Loading...**. The page change itself needs iOS 18.2 or later, or a recent Chrome; older ones load pages as before. Redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`); nothing changes on the race PC.
- **A flush, steady bar on a home-screen icon (1 November).** Since the bottom-bar fix of 31 October, the club's pages opened from an iPhone home-screen icon showed an empty band under the bottom bar, and the first fix for it let the bar jump again. An iPhone reports a page opened from its icon as shorter than the screen it draws on, so the bar now measures from the top of the full screen, and the page still scrolls inside itself so the bar never moves. In Safari and Chrome nothing changes. Redeploy the relay (`fly deploy --ha=false`); nothing changes on the race PC.
- **Paste a race result from LiveRC (1 November).** **Race History** has a new **Paste a Result...** button. Copy a race's result table off its LiveRC results page, press the button, check the class, round and day, and the race comes into Race History as **Imported**. It's kept for the record and never scores: no personal bests, records, badges, points or commentary, and it isn't sent to the online results. See [Pasting a result from LiveRC](race-control/results-and-championships.md#pasting-a-result-from-liverc). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Paste your LiveRC entry list (1 November).** On **Driver Profiles**, **Paste a Table...** now reads a whole LiveRC entry list copied off your own event's page: every class at once, each driver's class from the title over their table, **Transponder #** picked as the transponder, and the doubled names ("BLACK, KALEBKALEB BLACK") put back to one ("Kaleb Black"). The **#** column that only counts drivers is no longer taken for race numbers. See [From your LiveRC entry list](race-day/drivers-and-classes.md#import-drivers-from-a-spreadsheet). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Every picked driver gets the inspection (1 November).** On **Who's Racing**, picking drivers who weren't next to each other in the list and then double-clicking one of them could log the inspection, or the check-in, for that one driver only. A double-click on a picked driver now covers every driver picked, wherever they sit in the list, with or without <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> held, and a <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+double-click on a driver who isn't picked yet adds them to the rest. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Venue and Track layout stay dark on a closed race day (1 November).** On **Race Day → Setup**, a race day that can't be edited (a closed day, or the old **Before events** entry the page falls back to after you delete a race day) showed **Venue** and **Track layout** as two bright white boxes. They now keep the theme's colors in every look, like the drop-downs beside them. The same goes for every drop-down you can type in when it's switched off. Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **Pick several drivers on Who's Racing (1 November).** Hold <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> and click drivers, or <kbd>Shift</kbd>+click a run of them, then double-click any of the picked rows: **Check In *n* Drivers** ticks them all in, and **Log Inspection for *n* Drivers...** records one inspection for every car, each in the class its **Tech** cell shows. **Inspect Selected Cars** does the same from the button. A click with <kbd>Ctrl</kbd> or <kbd>Shift</kbd> held only picks the row, so it never ticks anyone by mistake. See [Several drivers at once](race-day/events-and-entries.md#several-drivers-at-once). Update HeatCaller on the race PC; nothing changes on the relay.
- **How it's written, first on AI Commentary (1 November).** The **Writing style** card is now called **How it's written** and is the first card on **Voice → AI Commentary**, since its tone, length and variety cover every line the model writes. Its settings are the same. See [How it's written](announcer/commentary.md#how-its-written).
- **Tests pass on any date (1 November).** Three checks of the AI commentary's monthly allowance started failing the moment October ended, because part of each test read the real date while the rest ran on a fixed day in October. They now all run on the test's own clock, and new checks cover the switch back to the relay at midnight UTC on the 1st, at the year end and after February. The app itself already handled the new month correctly, so nothing changes for clubs, on the race PC or on the relay.
- **Between races: call-ups, a say in the gap, and new flag buttons (31 October).** The time after the recap show and before the next race now has settings of its own, and Race Control has buttons for it. See [Between races](announcer/commentary.md#between-races).
    - **Call-ups by themselves.** The announcer now calls the next race to the drivers stand without anyone pressing anything: "Heat 3 for Stock, you're up next. Head to the drivers stand.", and the marshals with it. It repeats every 2 minutes, up to 3 times a race. **Automatic call-ups are on from this update**: untick **Call-up: next race to the drivers stand** on the new **Between races** card to stop them. They're written by HeatCaller, so they cost nothing and work with commentary off.
    - **How much, how often, when, and what.** The **Between races** card on **Voice → AI Commentary** sets how much is said between races, how long a line can run, the space between lines, the quiet after the recap and before the call, and which kinds of line play: call-ups (next race and two out), marshals, drivers not checked in, the hand-over, Coming up next, segments, club notes and the countdown. **The call-up goes first**: nothing the commentary writes is said in the gap until the next race has been called up once.
    - **Presets.** **Club night**, **Race day**, **Big event** and **Just the call-ups** set the whole card in one pick, and **Between-race talk:** on **Race Day → Setup** picks one for a single day.
    - **Your own lines.** Type your own call-up, marshal call or notices, with `{round}`, `{class}`, `{drivers}`, `{marshals}`, `{minutes}` and `{track}` filled in. **Put in Examples** starts you off.
    - **Hear the Gap** plays a sample gap in under a minute and writes down what would be said when.
    - **The countdown by itself.** Tick **Count down by itself from the run sheet** and the announcer counts down to each race from the time on the Timetable, at the minutes you pick, without pressing **Start**.
    - **New flag row after the flag.** **Skip**, **Hold Talk**, **Call Up**, **Edit Result**, **Reset**. **Skip** stops the line on air (hold it to end the whole recap show), **Hold Talk** holds everything between races until you press **Resume Talk**, and **Call Up** calls the next race now; right-click it to push the next race back a minute, call up two races out, the marshals only, or drivers not checked in. **Next Heat** and **Results** are gone: **Start Next** on the Next Up bar does what Next Heat did, and **Edit Result** opens the same race Results did. Untick **Show Skip, Hold Talk and Call Up on the flag row after the flag** to keep the old row. See [The flag row after the flag](race-control/running-a-race.md#the-flag-row-after-the-flag).
