- **The night is quiet early and loud late.** The airtime ceiling used to be a flat six spoken segments per ten minutes from the first practice session of the afternoon to the last lap of the A-main — so the pass for the win arrived with the same shrug as heat three. It now follows your program: three in practice, four in a heat, six in a B-main, nine in the A-main, and two more than that for whatever is last on the program tonight. The second voice goes the same way, held for the mains, so a room learns without being told that two voices means this one counts. A club with no program keeps the flat six and the second voice exactly as before — nothing here takes the announcer away from a club that hasn't set a program up. They're ceilings, not targets: every money gate still sits in front of them, and the console says which one each race is on so a quiet heat reads as a decision rather than a fault.

- **The Big Screen no longer freezes when the first video advert of the night comes up.** Starting the video player means loading it from disk, and on a club PC that took forty-three seconds the first time — all of it on the window in front of the crowd, at the exact moment a commercial break began. The window froze solid, the freeze watchdog wrote a gigabyte of dump, the recap being written behind it was dropped for taking too long, and the advert finally appeared twelve seconds into the program that replaced it. The player is now started quietly in the background as soon as the Big Screen sees that the club has a video slide, so by the time a break asks for one it is ready. A break that arrives before it is ready runs the club's picture slides for that slot and says so in the log.

- **An advert that starts late is not shown at all.** If starting a video takes long enough for the commercial break to end, the advert is dropped rather than played over the recap — an advert talking over the night's own program is worth less to the sponsor than the same advert in the next break, which is where it goes. The log says how long a video took to start whenever it is more than a second.

- **A written sponsor read can no longer go out over a video advert.** Two adverts for two different sponsors over each other sells neither. The announcer's paid reads now also stand down while a sponsor's video is on the crowd's screen, and an ad-break read that is owed waits for the next real gap exactly as it does for a race.

- **Breaks now lead with a video even when a slide is already up.** The break used to ask for an advert only when the screen happened to be on the standings, which between races is about half the time — so a club that had loaded three videos saw one of them, once, all evening. A picture slide already showing has had its turn and comes round again; a video already playing still keeps the screen, because cutting an advert off part way is worse than anything else here.

- **The Big Screen says what each break leads with.** One line per break in the log: leading with a video, holding on one already playing, or no video to lead with and why — none loaded, or the files have gone. The report that started all of this came with a log that said nothing either way.

- **The colour now plays straight after the honours**, rather than after the pit-lane interview: the program reads result, honours, colour, pit lane, goodnight, so the three things about the night sit together and the interview and sign-off close it.

- **The mechanic is introduced as the team's technical lead.** The announcer hands over with the role as well as the name — "Guiseppi, the team's technical lead" — and where no name is typed, by the role alone, which is a better handover than the bare "the winner's mechanic" it used to be. The setting on the Commentary page is still called the mechanic's name, because that is what you call the person when you are typing one in.

- **Why the form guide kept landing after the show.** It was not where it was being *placed* — it was that the ladder refused to hand it over while the program was talking. Every segment has a minimum gap since the last spoken line, which is a good rule for filler between races and the wrong one inside a program, where back-to-back is the whole idea: the program's own previous line had always just closed that gap, so the segment was refused and then became due the instant the show went quiet. Asked from inside a program, that one gate no longer applies. Everything else still does — switched off, already said tonight, already said for this class, nothing new to say, the ten-minutes airtime cap, and the month's allowance.

- **A sponsor's video only plays in a commercial break now.** The slide rotation used to play whatever came next at whatever moment its timer fired, video or still — and a video has its own soundtrack. On one race the club's advert played twice: once in the gap before the break had started, and again seven seconds into the recap, talking over the announcer reading the result. That second one can't be fixed by setting levels, because a video with sound deliberately ducks the club's music and then speaks over the program. Videos are now held for the break, which is where a broadcast runs its adverts anyway. Out of a break the rotation steps over a video to the next **still** slide rather than showing nothing, so a club whose slides are mostly videos still gets a board; if every slide they have is a video, the standings stay up and the adverts get the screen the moment a break starts. Still slides are not affected at all — they have no sound, and a sponsor's logo up during a recap is exactly what they paid for.

- **The colour segments are part of the recap now, not three more broadcasts after it.** The form guide, the grid history and whatever else the announcer had due used to arrive *after* the program had signed off — each one opening a show of its own, with its own opener and its own music bed. A club heard the recap, the honours, "and that's a wrap", and then two more mini-broadcasts. They are now items inside the recap's own show, after the honours and before the sign-off, with the same mark between them that a show puts between its own parts: result, honours, pit lane, colour, goodnight. One program. Two of them at most, because past two a program about a ninety-second race becomes the reason the next heat is late. They are written while the recap is being spoken, so they cost no extra silence, and a race called to the grid drops them like everything else. **Segments during a race and before one are untouched** — a battle call over a running race and a round intro before one happen exactly where they always did.

- **The host signed off, and then threw to the honours.** Confusing, and it was half a fix rather than a new fault: the recap's sign-off was correctly held back to come last, but the instruction that tells the model *whether to sign off at all* was still only asking about the mechanic's interview. On a race with badges and no mechanic the model was told to "sign off the race", did so, and the host then handed over to the honours reader straight afterwards — a program ending and then carrying on. Anything following the recap now stops it signing off.

- **The show comes with music now.** Seven music beds and seven openers ship with HeatCaller, and a fresh install starts on one of each — so the opener and the bed are there the first time you tick the show on, instead of being a job you have to do first. Pick from the new drop-downs on each row under **The show's music** on **Voice → Commentary**: beds are three minutes each with tempo in the name (Opening Drive 132 BPM, Halftime Heat 92 BPM, Hard Count 150 BPM and so on), openers are the short hits that land in front of the voice. **Your own files still win.** Nothing about **Choose...** has changed: set a file of your own for a part and that is what plays, the drop-down for it greys out to say so, and **Remove** puts you back on the shipped piece rather than on silence. A club that had already dropped their own `opener.*` or `bed.*` into the show music folder is left exactly as they were. The sting — the mark between two parts of a show — deliberately starts at none, because nothing that ships is short enough to be a mark and a swell of the bed does that job better; you can still pick one of the openers for it if you want.

  All fourteen pieces were levelled to the same loudness on the way in. That matters more than it sounds: nothing in HeatCaller normalises audio, and the opener/bed/duck percentages were tuned against material at a particular level — so a hotter bed would have sat on top of the announcer rather than under him, and switching beds would have changed your whole mix. They are all the same now, so the levels you set by ear hold whichever bed you choose.

- **The pause when the second voice came in.** A two-hander — the honours reader, the co-host, the mechanic — had about a second and a half of dead air at every change of voice, which is enough to stop it sounding like two people and start it sounding like two announcements. Almost none of it was deliberate. Every line is padded with silence at both ends to stop its start or finish being clipped: 0.9s behind and 0.15s in front. Between two turns of one exchange both were being charged in full, and the deliberate beat at the change of voice sat on top of them — so the beat was a third of a gap nobody had ever measured. The pads are now trimmed to nothing between two lines that run straight into each other (the long one exists for exactly this and had never been used anywhere), and with them gone the beat is audible as itself for the first time, so it has come down from 450ms to 180ms — about where a person comes in on somebody else. **The gap at a change of voice goes from roughly 1.55s to about 0.25s.** The mechanic's interview gained the most: it has no beat of its own, so the whole gap between a question and its answer was padding, and it is now about a quarter of a second.

- **The announcer now has a name, and he introduces himself.** There is a new **Announcer's name:** box under **Broadcast** on **Voice → Commentary**. Type one and he welcomes the room and says who he is at the cold open and at the top of the recap - once, the way a presenter does, and nowhere else. Leave it blank and he is never named. This also fixes something odder: every other voice in the broadcast could already be named - the mechanic, the honours reader, the co-host - and the one voice carrying the whole program could not. So when a club ran the two-voice segments, the model was writing dialogue between a named person and an anonymous one, and it did the obvious thing: it gave him a name of its own and had the co-host use it on air. A club heard "And Asher's still within striking distance, Laura" twice in one race. There was no Laura. Where you leave the box blank, the model is now told plainly that the voice has no name and must not invent one.

- **The sign-off is the last thing you hear.** It was only ever held back for the mechanic's interview, and the honours were invisible to it - so on every heat the recap signed the program off itself ("and that's us, next up the A-Main, goodnight") and the badges were then read out afterwards. From the floor it sounded exactly like that: the honours arriving after the show had ended. Anything that follows the recap now pushes the sign-off out to its own line at the end. Nothing to set.

- **Two shows could play over each other.** Race Control runs the whole program after a flag - teaser, recap, honours, mechanic, sign-off - and the Announcer screen's director independently looks for a segment to fill the gaps between races twice a second. Nothing held the two apart. On a club night the recap opened its show, and nine seconds later the director opened the night's **sign-off** on top of it: two openers, two music beds, mixed together over one voice, and the program signing off before it had read the honours out. The post-race program now holds the broadcast for its whole length and the filler stands down while it does. Nothing is lost by standing down - a segment that goes unsaid is unspent - and the gap after a recap is long. Paid sponsor reads respect it too, including in the quiet stretches between the recap's own lines, which were a gap a read could previously drop into.

- **The announcer promised one honour too many.** The throw to the honours reader says how many are coming - "three to hand out" - and that count was the number of *awards* rather than the number of *badges*. The two differ whenever one badge goes to more than one driver, which the date-based badges do constantly: Weekend Warrior and Back to Back fire for everybody who raced both days. So a race where two drivers both took Weekend Warrior had the announcer promise three and the room then hear two badge names. It now counts distinct badges. Where the read's cap bites, the console reports both figures, so a read that comes out shorter than the log has an explanation.

- **Play again, and Skip, on Race Control.** The announcer strip under the Next Up bar had one button, **Hold**, which stops the *next* thing. Everything already said was final and everything in progress ran to its end whatever happened in the room. There are now two more. **Play again** puts the last segment out again, with its show round it - for a line the room talked over, a PA that cut out, or somebody who missed the honours. It costs nothing: no model request, nothing spent off your allowance, and it works with the AI switched off at the wall, which is exactly when you are most likely to want it. A two-voice segment goes out as two voices rather than as one voice reading a transcript. **Skip** stops the show that is on air - the bed rides down and the rest of the running order is dropped - and deliberately lets the sentence being spoken finish: cutting it off would mean clearing the voice queue, and that is where the checkered flag, the countdown and a driver's release call all sit.

- **The strip says which segment just played, not what it said.** It used to print the first ninety characters of the last line, in quotes. That was the wrong thing on the busiest screen in the app: you heard the line, or you were busy, and a wall of text in a one-row strip is not how anybody catches up - and it shoved the buttons sideways as it grew and shrank. It now reads "Last: Race Recap", "Last: Club Honours", "Last: Cold Open", which is what tells you where the show is up to. A two-voice segment says so.

- **Why your sponsor's video did not play after the race.** Three separate things, and the first was the real one. The commercial break gave up silently when **Play the sponsors as a commercial break while the recap is being written** was switched off - which is the likeliest reason of all, since it is off until you turn it on - so a club asking the question had a log with nothing about a commercial break in it anywhere. It now says so. Second, a break is usually about one turn of the slide rotation long, so with the slides taken in file order a club's single video advert came up on a break roughly never: the stills either side of it had the eight seconds. A break now **starts** on a video where you have one and fills the rest of itself with the stills, searching forward from wherever the rotation is so two videos take turns across the evening. Third, a video that did get its turn could be cut off part way, because the break ended as soon as the recap was ready: a video actually playing now holds the break open until it finishes, under the same 90-second ceiling, so you do not sell a thirty-second spot and show eight seconds of it.

- **Video adverts say what happened to them.** The video path had no logging at all. It worked out a careful reason for every way it could fail - the video player's files missing from beside HeatCaller.exe, the player refusing to start, a file it could not read - and then threw every one of them away, so a club whose advert did not appear could not tell "this PC cannot play video" from "that file is broken" from "the rotation never reached it". The Big Screen now reports which video is playing and whether with its sound, that video cannot play on this PC at all with the reason (said once, not every time the slide comes round), and which file the player turned away, by name.

- **"He was a second off this in practice."** The announcer can now compare a driver's race pace tonight with their **practice** pace tonight. This fills a real gap rather than repeating the figure that was already there: the existing one compares a race with a race, so it needs a driver to have been out twice and says nothing before the third race of a class, where this one needs one practice run and one race and so exists at the second. It is scoped as "in practice tonight" and never to a class, deliberately - a practice session has no class, so HeatCaller cannot know whether the driver practised the car they are now racing, and the line says only what is true. **Practice is still not a race:** it is read separately, and nothing else has started counting it - not the field strength, not the form guide, not the championship, and not a start in anybody's career. A club that does not run its practice through HeatCaller hears nothing about practice, exactly as before.

- **A recap that overran said nothing about it.** Each part of the post-race program waits up to 90 seconds for the one before it to be heard, then carries on regardless - which is right, because the honours and the sign-off must not be lost to a long recap. But it did it silently, so a night where everything after the recap arrived bunched up behind it looked, in the log, exactly like a night where each part was heard out properly. It now says so.

- **Five things the announcer offered and could never say.** Under **Segments** on **Voice → Commentary** there are tick boxes for things the announcer covers beyond the intros and recaps. Five of them did nothing at all. **Form guide** ("How the drivers in a main have been going lately."), **Halfway standings** ("Where things stand partway through the heats."), **This day at the club** ("What happened here on this date in other years."), **Cold open** ("Sets the evening out before the first race.") and **Sign-off numbers** ("The night's totals, fastest lap and closest finish.") each had a box you could tick and nothing behind it, so ticking one bought silence and nothing ever said why. All five work now. The form guide reads a driver's last few finishes in that class out in order, most recent first, so it is your own saved results rather than a verdict on anybody. The halfway standings go out partway through the heats, once or twice a night per class. "This day at the club" looks for what happened at your track on this date in earlier years. And the two ends of the evening finally exist: the cold open counts the night up before a wheel has turned - how many classes, how many drivers, how many races still to run, and where, if you typed a venue on the race day - and the sign-off reads back the numbers nobody in the room was counting: races run, laps turned, the fastest lap of the night and who did it, the closest finish, if there actually was a close one, and how many drivers raced at your track for the first time. Practice slots are left out of the cold open's count of races, deliberately: a night of ten heats and twenty practice sessions is ten races, not thirty.

- **The announcer has four new kinds of thing to talk about, and none of it is made up.** Race intros used to have the grid and very little else, so two races with the same ten drivers sounded the same. They now also get **the track's lap record** - the outright best on the layout you are running, who holds it and when it was set; **how strong the field is** - the biggest field this class has had at your club and how many races back that was, and how many of the drivers in this race have won a main in that class at your club this year; **how the evening is going** - what has happened to a driver's pace since their first race of the night, measured the same way as the track evolution figure on the **Next Up** bar so the two can never disagree; and **the car**, from the **Garage** - which only the mechanic's interview ever mentioned before, and which the recap now has as well, along with where tonight's result sits in the winner's own record ("that is his first win here", "he has never finished this high"). Every one of them is counted out of your club's own saved results, because a record a driver knows is wrong costs you the rest of what the announcer says. Where the app cannot count something, it says nothing instead of guessing.

- **The night is told as one story now.** The announcer picks one storyline at the cold open - a driver who has won the last race of the last few race days, say - refers back to it between races, and resolves it at the sign-off, so an evening has an arc instead of being nine unconnected races with announcements over them. Two things make it safe to listen to. The shape is invented but the claim inside it never is: "can anybody stop that run tonight?" is a question, and the run it is asking about is counted out of your filed results or there is no storyline at all. And nothing about it is remembered, which is the point - it is read fresh every time, so the moment somebody beats the driver the story was about, the story turns instead of carrying on asking a question the room has already answered. A night with no shape to it, or a club with too few race days or too few drivers on file, gets no storyline rather than a manufactured one.

- **The announcer can say what a race would decide - with the condition said out loud.** A new line in a race intro: *"if Sam wins this one and Bill is second, Sam takes the lead of the Winter Series from Alex, by three points."* It is only ever offered as a named finishing order, never as "a win here and he leads the championship", and the difference matters: what a result does to a table depends on where everyone else finishes, and a flat version of that sentence is true for one finishing order out of many. Before it says anything, HeatCaller scores the hypothetical through the same championship machinery your standings come from - your points scheme, your "best N of" drop rule, your excluded drivers - and then prices every other driver in the race at the best they could possibly do, including retiring, and checks the claim still holds. If it does not hold for every arrangement of the rest of the field, nothing is said. The margin in points is only given when it is the same margin whatever the rest of the field does; otherwise the line says the lead changes hands and gives no number. Level on points is not leading, so a hypothetical that only draws level is refused too. It needs a championship with a few rounds already counted and at least two of its drivers in the race, so it stays quiet far more often than it speaks - and when it does speak, the condition it was proved for is written in the console beside it, so you can account for anything the announcer said after the night.

- **The sponsors play as a commercial break while the recap is being written.** The AI recap takes the model a while to write - well over half a minute on a slower PC - and all that used to cover it was the teaser ("Give us a second and we'll tell you how that one unfolded."). Now, with **Play the sponsors as a commercial break while the recap is being written** ticked on **Voice → Commentary**, the finish call and the teaser are followed by a proper break: the Big Screen hands its rotation over to your sponsor slides and videos, the **ON AIR** band reads **COMMERCIAL BREAK**, and the recap comes back from it on your sting - which is what a sting is for. The break is exactly as long as the model took. It never runs under eight seconds, so it cannot look like a glitch, and never over ninety, so a model that has hung cannot keep the standings off the crowd's screen all evening; it ends early if it has been round every sponsor you have, and it is abandoned at once if the next race is called to the grid. Nothing is added for a club without it: with no sponsor slides, with them switched off, or with no Big Screen open, the night runs exactly as it did before. Your music keeps playing under the slides - a break of still pictures with no sound is dead air - and a sponsor video with sound quiets it as it always did.

- **Three smaller things in the announcer.** The show no longer opens before there is anything to open for: the music used to start before the recap arrived, so on a slow night the crowd heard the opener land, ring out, and then eighteen seconds of music bed with nobody talking over it. The opener now waits until the recap is written, and a race the model had nothing to say about opens no show at all. **Grid history** is retuned, now that race intros carry the same history themselves: it comes round less often, and a debut or a head-to-head is announced once an evening rather than once a race - six heats with the same ten drivers used to mean the same debut announced six times. And the console no longer fills up with one line: a segment the announcer has run out of budget for is now reported once, not on every half-second pass, which on a busy night was hundreds of copies of the same sentence burying everything else in the log.

- **The robotic Windows voice is gone, and Kokoro is the free voice for everyone.** **Voice → Voice Settings** no longer offers the Windows voice, and nothing falls back to it. Kokoro - natural-sounding, free, running on your own PC, costing nothing however much the announcer talks and needing no account and no internet - is what a new club gets and what speaks whenever the voice you chose can't: no ElevenLabs key, no Wi-Fi, a spent quota or a used-up HeatCaller allowance all land on Kokoro instead of going quiet. If your settings said **Windows voice** you come up on Kokoro with nothing to do, and your old pick of a Windows voice for the co-host or the mechanic is simply forgotten. **If you pay ElevenLabs by the character you stay on ElevenLabs**, deliberately: a club that had a key *and* had chosen the free voice is not moved onto the one that charges them. The one thing worth knowing is that Kokoro is now the bottom of the ladder, so if its voice file is missing from your PC the announcer says plainly - in the console, on the **Mixer** as a red NO VOICE, and on Voice Settings - that it cannot speak, and shows you the one button that fixes it.

- **Hear a voice before you choose it.** Voice Settings has a **Hear It** button beside the HeatCaller voice list: it plays a short sample of the highlighted voice, free, and it works *before you have signed up for anything at all*, so you can listen to every voice on offer and then decide. The first person anywhere to listen to a given voice has it made once; everyone after hears the saved copy for nothing. Beside it is a box for your own words and a **Preview** button, which has the announcer say your actual class names and driver names in that voice - because a stock sample cannot tell you whether it gets "Stock Buggy A-Main" right. Previews come out of a small daily allowance of their own, **kept completely separate from your race-night voice**: an afternoon of auditioning can never leave you short on Saturday, and when the day's previews are gone the app says exactly that rather than quietly spending the other budget. This closes the oldest item on the known-issues list. **The samples and the Preview button are served by the relay, so the relay needs redeploying before either works:** get the new relay code and run `fly deploy --ha=false`.

- **Check how a driver's name is read, before the night.** A driver's **Announcer name:** box has always been read instead of their real name; it now says plainly that you can spell it the way it *sounds* - put "Zhock" in and the announcer says Jacques properly, while he stays Jacques on the heat sheet, the results and the website. New beside the box: **Hear It**, which speaks the name exactly as the announcer will, in the voice your club is set up with, from what is in the box right now - no saving, no race. And **Check Names...** on **Driver Hub** opens a window that lists everyone the roster is showing, with a tick box to narrow it to whoever is checked in tonight, and reads them out one press at a time with the words it is saying on screen. Three wrong names get fixed in five minutes before the doors open instead of over the PA. **Hear It** is on the Add Driver window too, which is where a transponder nobody recognised gets named at seven o'clock.

- **A show is one piece now, with punctuation between its parts.** A show used to be a run of separate announcements that happened to have music under them. It is now a single block: it opens once, says its two or three things with a deliberate mark between them, and plays out. The marks are the three a radio station uses - your **sting**, a new third file beside **Opener:** and **Bed:** on **Voice → Commentary**, put in between two parts that have nothing to do with each other; the bed **swelling** for about a second where one thought leads into the next; and a plain beat of silence, about two thirds of a second, where the words themselves carry the change. Nothing is put in front of the first part, and no mark goes between every pair, which is exactly what would make it sound mechanical. A block is held to a minute and a half, and the joining lines are the first thing dropped if it would run longer - never a race call. And if a race moves on while a show is talking - staged, counting down, a green or any flag - the block gives up at once and the next thing the crowd hears is the race.

- **Two voices hand over to each other.** With a co-host set up, every change of voice inside a show now gets a beat of air - just under half a second - and the music bed nudges up underneath it, which is the sound of a studio handing over rather than two people talking over a loop. It happens inside a show only, so an ordinary race call is not slowed down by a fraction of a second.

- **HeatCaller writes the lines that join a show together.** The sentences that carry the crowd from one part of a show to the next - a look back at the race just run, what is still to come, the first race of the night, the last one - are written by HeatCaller itself, not by the AI model. So they cost nothing, need no setting up, and keep working when the model is slow, busy, unreachable or not installed at all. Each kind has several phrasings, drawn out of a shuffled bag so the same sentence is never said twice in one night, and the car count multiplies them again. Nothing in them is invented: the class names and the numbers come from the program you built and the results you filed, which is why they need a race day open with its program on **Race Day → Next Steps** and the commentary switch turned on. A quiet moment between two races can now be one of these lines on its own, carried on your own theme.

- **The show no longer sounds the same every time.** The first show of the night plays your opener from the top, as it always did - but the ones after it **come in on the tail of the same music**, so it is unmistakably your theme and it is over in about a second instead of four. That is how a radio station keeps a theme from wearing out over three hours, and it answers the complaint the guides themselves admitted to ("you'll hear the opener before every heat, which gets old over a long night"). A show after a quiet spell re-establishes the theme without making a meal of it, and the sign-off gets a full open and the bed held up on its own after the last word. When the announcer stops talking the music now comes **up** for a beat before it finishes, which is the sound of a program ending rather than the music running out, and a beat of air sits in front of the opener so it no longer lands in the same instant your playlist stops. One new tick box on **Voice → Commentary** if you would rather hear all of your opener every single time, and the page tells you, in your own file's seconds, exactly where a later show will come in.

- **The Big Screen says ON AIR while the show is on.** A band comes up across the foot of the Big Screen, under the ON DECK bar, the moment a show starts: a steady broadcast-red **ON AIR** block, and beside it on a plate what is being read - **RACE RECAP**, **THE GRID**, **STANDINGS SO FAR**, **SIGN-OFF** and so on. It wipes on from the left and then sits still. Nothing blinks and nothing crawls, because something moving while the crowd is trying to read the leaderboard is worse than no band at all. It delays nothing and covers nothing that matters - the leaderboard is the only thing that gives up any room, and everything else stays where it is, the grid roll call deliberately included, so a show over a staged race keeps both on screen. It comes in every look, with or without your logo, on every Big Screen you have open. Press <kbd>O</kbd> to turn it off, and unlike the track map (<kbd>M</kbd>) that choice is remembered between sessions. It needs your show music set up: with no opener and no bed there is no show to be on air with.

- **A sponsor can have a read, not just a mention.** The **Sponsors** page takes a few sentences of proper copy for each sponsor, beside their slides and their videos, with a live hint telling you about how long it takes to read. You choose where it is heard: at the end of an ad break once their video has finished, in the gap between races, both, or neither. The announcer reads your words exactly as typed, so it costs nothing to run and **works with the AI commentary switched off entirely**. Copy longer than the gap finishes in the next one rather than being cut off mid-sentence; a race call always comes first and a recap or a "coming up next" always outranks an advert; and if you sell four slots all four really get read - HeatCaller keeps count and shares them out by whatever share each sponsor bought.

- **Every race is saved as an mp3, with a clip for each driver.** HeatCaller records the race as it goes out - the announcer, the effects, the stingers and the sponsor reads, but not your music, which plays on its own and never passes through the part being recorded. When the race and the after-race chat are done it writes the whole thing to **Documents\HeatCaller Race Audio**, a folder per night, named with the date, the time and the race so a file from three weeks ago is obvious without opening it. It also cuts each driver a short mp3 of just the moments they were named - their fastest lap, where they passed somebody, a penalty, and for the top three the finish and the interview - so you can hand a driver their bit of the night. Those moments are worked out from the lap times already saved with the result, so there is nothing new to keep and nothing new to go wrong. It cannot hurt a race night: capped at forty minutes, it gives up by itself if the disk runs low, it never goes near the laps or the results, and if the PC is closed or crashes mid-race the part already recorded still plays and becomes an mp3 next time. **Race audio is deliberately left out of your backups** - it is far too big, exactly like the photo-finish pictures - so copy that folder yourself if you want it kept. There is no tick box for it yet: it is on, and the race log prints the exact folder it is writing to.

- **Close battles and record laps can be called again.** The two things that watch a race as it runs - the one that spots a fight holding for half a minute, and the one that holds a record lap until there is room to say it - were being wiped twice a second by the housekeeping that starts a new night. Both needed to accumulate for far longer than that, so **neither could ever fire in a real race**, while passing their own tests. They are reset per race now, as they always should have been.

- **The twenty build warnings are gone.** Eighteen of them were one complaint, repeated: the main window's constructor is about 1,490 lines long and the compiler gives up following it, then reports every store it could not follow as possibly unset. They are all set, unconditionally, and the warning is now turned off for that one constructor with the reasoning written down beside it - rather than by marking eighteen fields as nullable, which would have silenced every genuine warning about them for ever and told the next reader they can be null, which they cannot.

- **The sidebar narrows to just its icons.** Click the chevron beside the **Search pages...** box, or press <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>B</kbd>, and the sidebar folds down to a rail of icons - about a quarter of its width, with the rest going to the page beside it. On a 1366-wide laptop at the track that is a column of the leaderboard back. Nothing is hidden: every page is still there, in the same order and the same groups, the page you were on stays on screen, and dragging a page still reorders it. What goes is the words - the page names, the group headings, the search box and the **HEATCALLER** header, which becomes **HC**. Hover over an icon for its page's name, and for its count where it has one: the **Rentals** count becomes a dot in the icon's corner with the number in the tooltip. The three buttons at the bottom stack into a column. <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>F</kbd> opens the sidebar first and then puts the cursor in the search box, since the box is not there to type into. How you leave it is remembered, so a club that prefers the rail gets it at every start. It is the everyday version of Race Mode (<kbd>F11</kbd>), which hides the sidebar outright and shows only Race Control. See [Just the icons](getting-started/tour.md#just-the-icons).

- **Fixes from a fifth bug hunt.**
  - **The show, and the club's music coming back:** the club's music now always returns after a show. A round intro's show takes up to three seconds to open, and a race leaving staging inside that window - a countdown started by hand, a reset, a stop, a red flag - used to cut a show that wasn't there yet: the real one landed a moment later and was never ended, so the music stayed muted and the bed looped under the countdown, the horn and every heat for the rest of the evening. A show whose start is called off during the opener - the next race called to the grid over a recap's teaser - now stops its bed as well as giving the music back; it used to leave the bed looping under the playlist all night. A show beginning on one thread while another ends can no longer be reported in the wrong order and leave the music muted, and a fault in anything listening for a show no longer stops the show ending. Two shows open at once (the announcer's director and Race Control share one) no longer overwrite each other's view of whether the announcer is talking. An absolute path left in the show's opener or bed is ignored unless it is a real local path, so a planted network path can't have Windows log on to another machine when the **Commentary** page opens.
  - **Race night:** a sponsor video the grid roll call takes the screen from now stops. It used to keep playing: its sound over the start of the race, and the music muted for the whole heat. **Delete** on the program's right-click menu now names the rounds you've picked that have already been run and says their results stop counting; it took them while reassuring you nothing would be lost. **Close Day** asks first, says how many rounds haven't been run, what stops working and how to put it back, and refuses while a round is on track - as **Delete Day**, the button beside it, always did. **Open Big Screen** brings the window already up to the front instead of opening a second one over it. Undoing a deleted timetable line puts that line back, with its time, its place and its tick, and leaves the rest of the sheet alone; it used to take back everything done to the sheet since.
  - **Results and championships:** undoing a **Reset** no longer books the next crossing after the green as a lap as long as the stoppage. The race clock moved on but the decoder's clock and every driver's readings on it stayed where they were, so a lap the length of the stoppage and a jumped elapsed time went into the saved result and the journal. Championship standings now come out in the same order every time: two drivers exactly level on points, wins and best finish are settled on a count-back of podium finishes, where they used to fall in whatever order the night's races happened to be read in - which also made the end-of-race swing report show a place change for somebody whose points hadn't moved. **A championship with two drivers exactly level may re-order once with this build.** A second correction to a saved result no longer records the renumbered place as the one the race recorded, which could settle a tie the wrong way round.
  - **Timing, and the freeze:** HeatCaller no longer stops responding while it looks for a decoder. **Connect when HeatCaller starts** retries every few seconds, and the open itself - listing USB devices for an EasyLap, or closing and reopening a COM port for an I-Lap - ran on the window's own thread with no time limit. A decoder wedged on the bus with its device still listed, a Bluetooth serial port that takes half a minute to pair, or an adapter pulled out of its socket each stop inside the Windows driver with no error and almost no work being done, and the window stops with them. This is the leading explanation for the ten seconds rehearsal 2 lost in September, and for why HeatCaller watches itself for freezes at all. The opens now happen off that thread, one at a time. The I-Lap's raw capture also wrote each line from the decoder straight to disk while the window waited on the same lock to disconnect, and each lap was handled while holding it.
  - **Announcer and commentary:** the **Qualifying Report** names tonight's TQ. It counted every heat the class had ever run. The co-host now works on the model on the laptop: the two-hander's **A:** tag and its line breaks were stripped before the two parts could be told apart, so the exchange came out silently as one voice and paid for a second write on top. On a PC that writes decimals with a comma, lap times, gaps and margins went into the prompt as "12,345s" and came back out of the PA as "twelve thousand three hundred forty-five seconds". The director now cools off the battle it just called instead of picking another one, keeps its facts when a hold comes on part-way through a write instead of spending them, and works out every segment's facts before the first request goes out. **Green (Start)** pressed with nothing staged no longer reads the last race's recap over the green race: a flag now drops the recap along with the rest of the queue, where it alone used to be spared.
  - **Music and crowd sound:** the music and the crowd no longer stall the window while they open or close a sound device. Both held the lock the window reads whether the music is playing and whether the crowd is running through, several times a second, across opening the file, opening the output and closing it again - and opening an output device is the slow thing the app's shared sound output exists to avoid. A playlist whose files won't play now tries each one once and stops instead of going round again.
  - **Your data:** a settings file holding just `null` - four bytes, which a half-finished write or a sync tool can leave behind - is now treated as unreadable, so HeatCaller keeps a copy aside, holds its writes and says so. Race history, the race program, the driver roster, track layouts, handicaps and the music library used to read it as a fresh install without a word, and the first change of the night wrote an empty file over the club's data.
  - **Phones and Local Screens:** the Live Results page no longer gives away transponder numbers. Any phone on the club's Wi-Fi can fetch the spectator board with no PIN - that is what it is for - but every row on it, every car on the track map and the whole **Follow a driver** list, including everyone gridded in races still to come, was keyed by the driver's transponder. One request handed over a transponder beside a full name for the night's entire entry: the exact pairing the roster spends a check-in PIN to keep off the Wi-Fi. They now use the same anonymous id the roster uses. Two things follow from it: a phone following a driver forgets who it was following when HeatCaller is restarted, because the ids are new each time it starts; and the broadcast graphics page needs one refresh in OBS after this build, because it now tells a ghost from a real car by a mark on its row rather than by reading its id.
  - **The Track Designer:** fixes to the **Select** tool, found hours after it was written. **Duplicate** (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>D</kbd>) leaves what it copied whole - it put the copy one tile over the original, so putting it down ate the thing it was copied from. Holding an arrow key down now nudges the selection across the plan without eating what it travels over; it used to put the selection down on every press. <kbd>Esc</kbd>, or picking another tool, part-way through a drag now puts the pieces down where they are instead of leaving them stacked on top of whatever was underneath - invisible, counted twice by the parts list and the kit check, and saved that way. Typing the room's size is one <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>Z</kbd>, and typing the size it already is adds nothing to undo. The plan no longer says a layout doesn't fit the room when it fits turned the other way.

- **The Track Designer can pick part of a plan up and move it.** A new **Select** tool (<kbd>8</kbd>): drag a box round part of the plan and everything it covers comes up together — tiles, rails, turns, markings and racing-line points. Because the box snaps out to whole tiles, the rails round the edge of a block come with it rather than being left behind, which is what made sliding a layout across the room worth doing by hand before. Then drag it to move it, arrow keys to nudge it a tile at a time, **Turn** (<kbd>]</kbd>) to turn it a quarter clockwise, **Flip across** and **Flip down** to mirror it, <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>C</kbd> and <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>V</kbd> to copy it, <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>D</kbd> to duplicate it one tile along, <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>A</kbd> for the whole plan, <kbd>Delete</kbd> to take it off and <kbd>Esc</kbd> to drop it. Shift-drag adds to what's picked, or takes it back out. A turn or a mirror keeps the top-left corner of the box where it is, so turning a corner complex never slides it off the floor you had it lined up with — and a turn has to get three different things right at once, because a tile fills a cell, a rail lies along a seam and a turn rail sweeps a quadrant of a grid point. Every drag, nudge, turn and mirror is one press of <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>Z</kbd>; picking something up isn't an edit, so it takes no undo step at all. See [Draw the layout in the Track Designer](race-day/track-designer.md).

- **The Track Designer knows every RCP set, opens one as a starting layout, and checks the plan against your hall.** **Check against a kit** now lists all eight sets RCP sells rather than two. **Start from a kit** opens one as a plan you can change: each one lays exactly the tiles the box holds and fits inside the rails it holds, and it costs one <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>Z</kbd> if it wasn't what you wanted. The circuits are layouts *buildable from* each kit rather than copies of RCP's own — their published parts lists rule out the tight corners their photographs appear to show, so the presets use the big sweeps the boxes are actually built for. **The room it goes in** takes the width and depth of your hall in feet or meters, draws it round the plan, and says whether the layout fits turned either way, with how much room to spare or how much too big it is. Where RCP publish only a tile count for a set and not its rails, the kit check says so rather than inventing a rail count to check against. Rails are also now drawn with the gray plastic bolts that hold them to the floor tiles, because that is what holds them on the real thing, and the plan says if a rail has no tile under it to bolt to.

- **The show can be scored with your own music.** The segments that open and close the evening — the cold open and the sign-off — can have a piece of music in front of the voice and another running underneath it. The opener lands, the bed comes up under it, and the first words arrive **over the opener's tail** rather than after it, so it sounds like a broadcast coming on air instead of a clip followed by an announcement. The bed sits under the whole read, drops a little further whenever the announcer is actually speaking, and when the read finishes it hands over to the piece's own fade and plays out. Your club's playlist goes quiet while it runs and comes back after; the crowd is left alone, because crowd noise under a broadcast open is the point. You choose the two files on **Voice → Commentary** and HeatCaller copies them into the club's data, so a backup carries them to the next laptop. The bed is looped — anything from about half a minute up works — and HeatCaller finds the fade on the end of it, keeps it back as the show's ending, and crossfades the loop's join, so you don't have to edit the file or find a loop point. **Play It** runs a real show with a test line over it, which is how the levels are meant to be set: by ear, not by numbers. Because it's your own music rather than something shipped with the app, no two clubs sound the same. See [The show's music](announcer/commentary.md#the-shows-music).

- **Sponsor slides can be videos, and the music gets out of their way.** The **Sponsors** page has an **Add Video** button: MP4, M4V, MOV, WEBM, MKV, AVI and WMV, in the same rotation as the pictures and in whatever order you put them. A video plays to its own end rather than for the slide time, because cutting a sponsor's 30-second spot off after eight seconds would be worse than not showing it, and while one is on screen with sound the music and the crowd bed fade out and fade back when it finishes — the announcer still speaks, since a race call always wins. Both of those are switches on the page if you'd rather they didn't. HeatCaller carries VLC's own decoders inside it, so a file that plays on your laptop plays on the track PC with nothing to install. Sponsor videos reach the Wi-Fi results page too, always muted there, because no phone browser will start sound on a page nobody has tapped. See [Videos](screens/big-screen.md#videos).

- **Cloud backup now tells you at a glance whether it's working.** **Backup & Restore** and **Mission Control** show one line with a color: green when every folder you picked got tonight's copy, red when none of them did or the last attempt failed, amber when it's only partly working, has never copied, or hasn't copied in over two weeks. Before, you had to read the dates and work it out yourself.

- **The app now says what's inside it and where to get help.** **Backup & Restore**'s **Getting help** card has a new **About HeatCaller...** button: the build you're on, what HeatCaller is, where the guides are, and every piece of software HeatCaller ships with and the license it's under. The same card now shows the support address, **support@heatcaller.com**, as a link with **Copy Address** beside it, and the box that appears when something goes wrong names it too, after **Save Support Info**. Before, both told you how to make the support file and not who to send it to.

- **One version number.** The version comes from one place in the project itself now, so the title bar, and later the installer and the updater, can never claim different builds. It used to be typed in a second time by hand.

- **Fixes from a fourth bug hunt.**
  - **Racing:** a car seen on the line during the countdown but behind it at the green no longer starts a lap ahead. Its first crossing just after the green is its start lap, as for everyone else. **+1 Lap** that brings the leader to the lap limit now puts the checkered flag out; the race used to run on and give the leader a lap too many. Added under a red flag, it brings the checkered out at the restart. **-1 Lap** on a phantom lap that brought the flag out early lets that driver's real last lap take it. With the announcer muted, the automatic start now gives staging and the countdown their **Stage (s):** and **Countdown (s):** lengths instead of going green almost at once. **Yellow** is now refused during practice, from the button, F4 and the phone, as **Red** already was. A yellow used to leave practice under a caution nothing could clear, with the lap calls held.
  - **Race filing:** pressing **Stage** again to restart a **Quick Race** or a re-run before its green keeps it on its own in **Race History**. So does starting a re-run's race from its warm-up with **Countdown** or **Green (Start)**, or getting a re-run back after a crash. Each used to file the race under the drivers' next heat.
  - **Heats and mains:** **Redo Remaining Heats** after part of a round has run deals that round's remaining heats only the drivers who haven't raced it yet. It used to call drivers who had just raced Q2 Heat 1 for Q2 Heat 2 as well. It also mixes the drivers between rounds when a class splits evenly into heats, as building the heats does. In a best-consecutive-laps class that counts more than one heat, drivers who never completed a full run are ranked on their best heats rather than their earliest.
  - **Results and championships:** the **Event Report**'s results, fastest laps, corrections and lap detail name the driver who raced, even if their transponder has since changed hands. Two drivers who shared a transponder on the day used to come out as one row under the new owner's name. Turning off **Add new races to this championship automatically** keeps the races already counted instead of emptying the table and the online standings, and races you untick while it's on stay out when you change the rules. Turning it back on hands its races back to the rules. Deleting a race day with its records cleared also clears a sector record set on a lap that **Edit Result** later merged.
  - **Badges:** **Record Holder** goes to the driver whose lap is the record shown on **Track Layouts**. A phantom lap Race Control refused no longer earns it or blocks it for good, and the first record after **Reset Record** earns it again. A car that never left the line no longer stops a **Comeback Kid**, or counts as an opponent for **Knows Everyone** or **Old Friends**.
  - **Ghosts and practice:** a ghost switched off during a race or practice now leaves the board as well as the track. Its frozen row used to stay there: the track map showed it as a stopped car, the leader was called as lapping it, everyone ahead of it got **Ghost Buster** or **Shadow Boxer**, and the race waited up to a minute after the checkered for it. A ghost switched on part-way through a race doesn't count for those badges. Ghosts switched on while a practice is running join it straight away. A ghost set to **A driver's recent average pace** leaves out the wait before the first lap and stops such as a battery change, so one built from a practice no longer runs far slower than the driver. In practice, the speed columns and the live track map use the track picked on the Practice page instead of Race Control's.
  - **Announcer:** a ghost's quicker lap no longer turns a real driver's race-fastest lap into a personal best call, and with **Announce ghosts like real drivers** off, a ghost on a long clean run no longer blocks the longest-streak call. Warnings and black flags have their own sound, **Warning or black flag** on **Sounds → Effects**, on for new installs and played even under a yellow. They used to borrow **Invalid lap**, which is off by default and was held or dropped like a lap chime. After updating, the new sound starts the way **Invalid lap** was set (the same sound, on only if that was on); if you'd never changed your sounds, it starts on, and the built-in soundscapes have it on. **Re-record all** on a soundscape's **Voice Lines** menu asks your ElevenLabs voice for new takes instead of handing back the saved ones. **LAST RACE SUMMARY** and **Repeat Race Summary** only offer the recap of the race that just finished, never an earlier race's.
  - **Music:** **Music after the recap** on the **Mixer** does nothing, and is grayed out, while **Start for:** **Races** is unticked. Music started by hand used to be faded or stopped after every recap anyway. The **When it ends** card no longer says audio you started by hand keeps going: **At the flag, stop:** and **Music after the recap:** end whatever is playing.
  - **Kokoro voice:** the first Kokoro line of the night no longer freezes HeatCaller while Kokoro gets its voices ready. A canceled or failed download no longer leaves a partly downloaded file of up to 310 MB behind. A download keeps going, with its progress and **Cancel Download**, when a soundscape, venue or **Lap times only** is applied, and pressing **Download** again no longer fails. A model that won't load offers **Download Again** instead of going back to saying Kokoro is ready a minute later.
  - **Timing:** **Connect** opens an I-Lap split loop on the COM port picked on the **Connection** page, not the one it used before. A split loop "Waiting for the I-Lap on COM*n*" connects by itself as soon as that port appears; heats used to run on without sector times. The **Welcome to HeatCaller** wizard no longer picks the last COM port listed for the I-Lap and remembers it, which could make **Connect when HeatCaller starts** open some other device. Its **Rescan** picks the port that appears when you plug the I-Lap in. Lining up the split loop no longer starts practice when practice starts on a gate crossing, which then refused Line Up's **Save**.
  - **Check-in and the timetable:** the timetable ticks off each heat as it's recorded, even while its window is shut, so ON DECK on the Big Screen, the Announcer screen, phones and the online schedule move on to the race that's really next. **Inspect Selected Car** opens the class the driver still needs inspecting in, and switching an inspection to another class starts its checklist fresh instead of copying the other car's ticks. Saving an inspection already on file under another class now asks first, because it moves the inspection out of the class it was in. **Who's Racing** and the phone count tech only for the classes racing tonight (and the **Class:** picked). Editing a timetable line keeps a time typed as text, such as "TBD".
  - **Race days:** **New Race Day...** on the phone manager starts a race day like **New Race Day** on the laptop, carrying over the kind of night, classes, venue, track layout, championship and heat settings. It used to make a bare day, and the next race day on the laptop copied its blanks. **Set Up Tonight** opens on your last race day's kind of night and names it to match. Its check-in ticks are timed like the desk's, so a slower online tap can't undo them, and the **Classes** step is grayed out until a day is open, where its choices used to be quietly thrown away.
  - **Drivers and classes:** importing the same driver list again no longer moves a driver who races two classes out of their first class, and a second row with a different **Car** adds that car to their garage. Undoing a class rename is refused, with the reason in the console, if a new class has taken the old name; it used to leave two classes with the same name. The undo also puts back the main or other class of a driver who raced a class with the new name too. Undoing a quick **Photo** change in **Driver Hub** no longer changes the photo of a different driver given the same transponder since.
  - **Garage and transponders:** **Transfer** on a car lists any races already run where a transponder going with it was timed as a car of its own, and asks before they count for the new owner. It used to move them without a word, while the window said races already run stay with whoever ran them; the **Transfer Transponder** window no longer says that either. A transponder fitted to a car that has since been retired uses the driver's active car for lap calls, the board and saved results, and a retired car transferred to someone doesn't become their active car.
  - **Phones and Local Screens:** the phone remote, the race-day manager and the Wi-Fi check-in page no longer pile up requests on weak Wi-Fi. A flag button on the phone remote says "Connection lost" within 12 seconds instead of staying on "Sending…". **Edit result…** on the manager asks for each driver's total laps added, and says "Nothing to change" rather than "Done" when what you type matches the result as it stands. The Big Screen's sponsor slide no longer comes back over the board at the green after the roll call, and the Live Results page skips a slide it can't load instead of blanking the standings. After a backup is restored onto another laptop and its keys can't be recovered, Wi-Fi check-in stays closed until a check-in PIN is set on **Local Screens**, including after a restart. It used to open to everyone on the Wi-Fi as soon as the keys were given up or any other secret was saved, and the page now says to set a PIN rather than to unlock the old one. A new staff, Remote, check-in or Manager PIN that is already some racer's personal PIN is refused.
  - **Club website:** through the relay, the race-day manager no longer shows whole transponder numbers. **Today** says how many changes can be undone on the laptop instead of listing them, and **Bump Ups** and the "already in use" messages name a car with no driver name by the last three digits of its transponder. Heat line-ups on the **Schedule** page appear only when the relay allows the club's roster, the same rule the roster follows. A check-in page left open when PINs change no longer counts as wrong guesses that could lock the venue's Wi-Fi out of PIN sign-in. Results already online no longer drop out of the archive when a re-send can't be queued, and **Publish again** on an earlier year's result puts that year in the archive and sends the standings again. The line-ups and check-in fixes are in the relay itself, so the relay needs redeploying: get the new relay code and run `fly deploy --ha=false`.
  - **Your data:** if the music library, the ambience, the transponder health record, the sign-up invites or the record of published results can't be read when HeatCaller starts (a backup or sync tool has it open, say), it's no longer written over. Your tracks, ambience layers, up to 20 nights of signal history, every invite, or whole race nights in the online archive used to be lost. Invites that couldn't be saved are no longer emailed or put online, and an invite canceled or given a new code while emails go out isn't marked as emailed. A save Windows can't finish because another program has the new file open is moved into place once it can be, or kept beside it under a temporary name; it used to be deleted along with the old file, which could wipe race-history.json. Typing a key or PIN while a restored backup's keys wait for the next start no longer throws those keys away; a **Keys not recovered yet** message now always says so, and that what you type meanwhile works until HeatCaller closes but isn't saved. Photo-finish pictures are left out of backups, which used to copy them all again every time until the backups could fill the disk; copy the `photo-finish` folder yourself to keep them. Half-written backups left by closing HeatCaller during a backup are cleared away.
  - The relay hosting and online guides give the right limits (results are kept until the club's space is full, not just the newest 300) and the right folder for the HeatCaller voice's files.

- **More fixes from the code audit.**
  - **Results and race filing:** running a heat again after it was recorded now saves the re-run on its own instead of filing it under the drivers' next heat. Undoing a Reset or Stage during a Quick Race (or recovering one after a crash) keeps it a Quick Race, so a shakedown is never filed as a heat. A race can no longer appear twice in Race History. Making a different race day current while a heat is running no longer drops the heat's round or changes its track. Looking at a past race day no longer files tonight's races into that day's heats.
  - **Race clock:** the race clock, time limit and the wait for stragglers are no longer thrown off when Windows syncs or changes the PC's clock mid-race.
  - **Laps and records:** removing a lap or undoing a correction no longer lets a jump-starter's short dash from the grid become the fastest lap, a track record or a personal best. Undoing a track-record reset no longer replaces a faster record set since. Cars that never turned a lap no longer count as beaten or as grid places for badges.
  - **Race-night safety:** choosing a new look while a race or practice is running no longer restarts HeatCaller; it offers to switch at the next start. A RED or YELLOW flag sent from a phone is no longer refused while another command is being carried out. Mission Control's next-race check counts a car only if it has been heard since the last race ended. When the split loop connects by itself it uses only the I-Lap's remembered COM port and never saves a different one over it.
  - **Practice and deleting:** saving a practice and then stopping no longer saves it twice, and laps after the first save count toward badges. Undoing the delete of a race that hadn't been saved yet puts it back in line to be saved normally. Deleting a race day also removes the earlier run of any heat that was run again.
  - **Club website:** results that couldn't reach the website before the laptop was shut down are now sent at the next start, and are only counted as online once the relay has them. Restoring a backup or fixing a mistyped club id no longer makes earlier race days disappear from the online archive, and correcting an old race no longer lists months that were never published. Unticking **Also publish the roster** now takes the roster off the website. Replies to the online race-day manager show only the last three digits of an unnamed car's transponder. The club home page uses a tiny fraction of the data it did, the race-day manager page no longer floods the relay when the laptop is slow to answer, and editing an earlier season's result no longer removes the current season from the archive.
  - **Saved data:** if the lap-beep, rivalry, sponsor, sound-effect, ghost or chassis-sheet file can't be read when HeatCaller starts, your saved settings are no longer replaced by your first change, and a damaged chassis-sheet file no longer throws away every edit. The one-time step that fills the Garage waits for a launch where its files can be read. Backups no longer include earlier backups, so they stop doubling in size. Restoring a backup now sets aside data the backup didn't contain instead of mixing it in (it is kept in the pre-restore safety backup).
  - **Voice and sound:** saving Voice Settings no longer resets the priority of Provisional Pole, Streak Lead, Close Battle, Lapped and the ghost calls, and settings already affected are repaired. ElevenLabs key, model, language and format are applied when you leave the box, not on every keystroke. If the recorded-lines list can't be read at startup, HeatCaller tries again shortly instead of speaking every line live all night. Lines rendered ahead of time show times as numbers on screens. **Re-record all** keeps a line's previous recording if its new one fails. Ambience **Preview** no longer silences a running race crowd. Music tracks you removed no longer come back at launch, and tracks you exclude stop coming up in the current playlist pass.
  - **Elsewhere:** on a sign-up, switching from a rental transponder back to "their own" restores the driver's own number. A tech inspection started from Check-In fills in the class and car. Exported results show the driver who raced, even if the transponder has since changed hands. Moving a timetable line past a round moves it one place. Closing the first-run setup with the X keeps what was entered on the open step. Opening an old setup entry and switching it to another chassis sheet keeps its values.

- **The Kokoro voice: natural-sounding and free.** **Voice → Voice Settings** has a new choice in **Speak with**: **Kokoro voice**. It runs on the laptop itself, so after a one-off download (about 310 MB, with the **Download** button beside **Kokoro voice:**) it needs no account and no internet, and nothing is ever billed however much the announcer talks. Pick from American and British voices and press **Test Kokoro Voice** to hear one. Until it's downloaded, or if it can't say a line, the Windows voice says it, so a flag call is never silent. Applying a style keeps your Kokoro voice, and the mechanic in the recap's interview can have one too. See [Use the Kokoro voice](announcer/voice.md#use-the-kokoro-voice).

- **A driver can race more than one class.** A driver's profile has a new **Also races:** line. Click **Choose...** beside it and tick the other classes they race besides their main one. They're dealt into those classes' heats too, listed under each class they race, shown with a chip for every class in **Driver Hub**, and found by **Find** under any of them. On the phone race manager, the driver list, its class filter and the racing-today counts take in every class they race, and setting a driver's class there changes their main class only. The check-in desk and the phone judge tech inspection across every class they race, as Race Control does, so a driver cleared in one class but not the other no longer shows as cleared and then holds up the start. **Import from CSV** reads a second row for the same driver in a different class as one of their other classes instead of replacing the first. Renaming a class renames it in everyone's other classes too, and Undo puts it back. See [A driver in more than one class](race-day/drivers-and-classes.md#a-driver-in-more-than-one-class).
- **The decoder connects by itself.** A new **Connect when HeatCaller starts** tick box on the **Connection** page, on by default, connects the line decoder (and the split loop with it) as HeatCaller opens, on the decoder and port used last. An I-Lap is only ever connected on the port it was last connected on. If that port isn't there yet (a USB adapter plugged in later), HeatCaller waits and connects as soon as it appears. Pressing **Connect** or **Disconnect** yourself takes over. Before, only an EasyLap connected by itself. See [Connect when HeatCaller starts](timing/decoders.md#connect-when-heatcaller-starts).

- **Fixes from the bug hunt before the club beta.**
  - **Timing:** an EasyLap pass that loses a byte on the way in no longer turns into a lap for a transponder nobody owns while swallowing the next car's pass. The damaged pass is dropped and the next one is read. A garbled clock reading as a decoder connects can no longer throw the decoder's clock out by days. An I-Lap whose first read failed the moment it connected no longer shows a green light with nothing reading the port. **Connect via:**, **Split loop:** and Line Up can't be changed while a race or practice is running: one mis-click or turn of the mouse wheel used to disconnect the decoder timing the heat without a word. HeatCaller now puts the choice back and says why.
  - **Split loop:** **Line Up** ignores a car sitting over both loops, which used to give a confident, made-up answer, and gives no answer until enough crossings agree. Its **Save** button is no longer off the bottom of the window. Moving the line to the other decoder while the split loop is off now turns the Line Up offset round too. It used to stay the old way round, putting every split out by twice the offset in sectors that still added up to the lap. The first sector record on a layout can't come from a split read early, and one early read no longer silences **Fastest sector** for the rest of the race. After a split loop is moved, **Record pace at the split** no longer compares with a record taken where the loop used to be. A split transponder claimed by two drivers shows as unknown at the split instead of quietly going to one of them, and undoing a driver edit or bringing back a deleted driver is refused when one of their split transponders has gone to someone else since. "Split loop not connected" stays on the Next Up bar for 15 seconds, then is left to the console and Mission Control, instead of turning the bar into a red alarm all race.
  - **Racing:** a jump start no longer sets the race's fastest lap. The dash from where the car sat to the line used to count as a lap that no real lap could beat, and was saved as a personal best and a track record. A car still waiting for its release, parked on the loop through a red flag, is no longer booked as a jump start when racing resumes. A transponder transferred to another driver mid-heat keeps its laps in the row it started in. A red flag in practice is refused, from the button, <kbd>F5</kbd> or the phone, which now says so: it used to stop practice laps counting with no green to clear it. A car on its warm-up laps no longer starts practice, which cancelled the warm-up and the heat waiting to stage. Undo of **Reset race** is refused once another race has started, so the next heat is no longer filed under the old round. Ghosts switched off mid-race now leave the track at once, and while ghosts are racing, switching one on or off adds or removes it straight away. They used to keep lapping, and being called, until the checkered. While real cars are on track, a ghost's lap is no longer shown as the session's fastest on the Big Screen, the Race Control board, the stat strip, **Practice** or the Live Results page.
  - **Heats and mains:** **Add Main** offers a third main as "C-Main" rather than "Main 3", which sat outside the bump-ups, and "A Final" is now read as a tiered main. A main added by hand goes in front of the higher mains still to run, so its finishers can bump up, and HeatCaller says when it's too late for that. Redoing the remaining heats keeps drivers who were added to a heat by hand from another class. With auto-advance following the class that was racing (the default), a race started with nothing picked in the Round box is filed against the heat whose roster holds the cars that were on track, not guessed from their classes. A driver added to a heat from another class keeps their "not in this class" mark when the heat is opened again. **Qualifying Standings** sorts the time, lap and heat columns by their figures, not as text, so "10.123s" no longer sorts above "9.500s".
  - **Results and records:** the event report has a **Status** column for DNF and DQ and lists the corrections made after the race, and the results card image shows DNF and DQ too. The card also says how many drivers it leaves out ("+3 more not shown, including 1 DQ"). When Edit Result takes off a phantom crossing that set the track record, the console now says the record still stands and to clear it on **Track Layouts**, and deleting the race day with records cleared now finds that record. Deleting a race day no longer clears a track record set tonight while the delete window was open, and undoing the delete makes the day current again only if it was current then. On **Pace**, a driver with fewer than three laps no longer tops **Best 3 in a row** or **Best 3 any**. A **Points** cell on the **Championship** page refuses anything but a whole number, instead of saving "1O" as 0. **Import from CSV** says which rows' points weren't numbers instead of dropping them. In the handicap suggestions, ticking a row after sorting the list credits that row's driver. Transferring a transponder that was timed as a car of its own in races already run now lists those races and asks before it moves, since they count for the new owner from then on. The **Transfer** window used to promise that races already run stay with whoever ran them. See [Transfer a transponder](race-day/drivers-and-classes.md#transfer-a-transponder). Duplicating a setup logged before chassis sheets keeps everything it says.
  - **Badges:** cars that never turned a lap no longer make up the field, so one car lapping a hand-released heat while the rest sat on the line no longer earns **Winner**, **On the Box** or **Full House**. A badge whose save failed is no longer announced, which used to call "First Timer" for the same drivers after every race.
  - **Announcer:** provisional pole goes to whoever tops the class's qualifying order (most laps then time, or the best run of laps in a row), checked on every crossing. It used to go to the quickest single lap, so a driver fifth on the board could be called P1 while the real leader never was. With **Announce ghosts like real drivers** off, a ghost on top no longer hides a real driver taking the lead, and is never named as a rival or chaser. In a most-laps qualifier, a lead change and the pole call no longer both speak for one pass, and qualifying heats no longer render a "go!" line for every car at the green. Round intros and the mechanic's interview no longer go out shouting: a lone word of four or more capitals, a hyphenated one ("HIGH-Octane") and a stretched one ("TIIIIIME") are calmed as well as runs of capitals, while short initials like "RC" stay. Team and sponsor names in a round intro keep their own spelling. With **Mention up to** set to 1, the recap no longer says there was no contest. A recap cut off by the next race is no longer offered to repeat on the **Announcer** page, and its writing stops so the next round's intro isn't held up. A recap spoken without being prepared ahead now shows on the Big Screen and the Announcer page's feed. A flag call made with the ElevenLabs quota spent now shows the quota as gone on the **Mixer** instead of filling the console with error text. When **Countdown started** is switched on on **Voice → Announcements**, the console says why it isn't spoken (the countdown is sound only, so the green isn't held up).
  - **Online and phones:** flags from the phone race manager's **Race** tab go one at a time through the same limit as the flag remote: RED tapped five times on slow Wi-Fi used to throw five red flags. A sign-up approved on a club rental, picked or typed by its number, is lent the unit by name with no profile on it, which used to put every later borrower's laps under that driver's name. A rental that's already out or retired is refused before anything changes. A re-sent sign-up answer can no longer hand a phone another driver's name and check-in link. The warning about invites made on another PC stays while any of them is still out. Sign-up codes made while the settings couldn't be saved no longer turn "invalid" for good at the next launch. On the club website, standings and the results archive come back after the relay restarts instead of staying empty until the next save, a correction made while the relay couldn't be reached is sent later, and the archive no longer lists races run while publishing was off, or the club's whole history before anything was published. Re-sending one old result from the phone no longer pulls every race since into the archive, and the re-sent result can be found there.
  - **Your data:** a data file left empty (by a sync tool, say) is now reported in the console instead of loading silently as a fresh install.
